# Review log: Name who starts the next session's runtime on a recycled pod

## Standing context

### Settled

- The runtime process lives as long as the pod on every pool and in both deployment models (human decision, 2026-09-30). No close at occupancy zero, no retirement keyed on deployment model, and no runtime redial.
- The adapter rule is unconditional. The adapter reads no recycle, acknowledgment, or cross-tenant setting, because the recycle `Shutdown` follows the last slot's close on the concurrent path; every non-reuse case is a gateway retire or a pool-admission refusal (D5).
- Process reuse requires the existing `acknowledgeProcessLevelIsolation` (human decision, D5), enforced at pool admission for a recycling pool with `maxSessionsPerPod > 1` outside `vm-restart` (spec-changes §12, decision 2). No boundary gate and no `process_reuse_unacknowledged` reason exist.
- On `allowCrossTenantReuse` pools, a pod is drained when a different tenant's session would be assigned to it (human decision, D6).
- A runtime that has stopped at a boundary retires the pod through the adapter's `runtime_live` report (human decision, D4). The fail-fast `Start` covers only a runtime that stops after the report.
- The generation never resets inside the pod's life, and `soleSession` requires its session to be live (D9).
- The new `Decide` input `RuntimeNotLive` is true to retire; the wire field is positive (D13).
- No path sends the `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` `terminate` frame, at a session's teardown or at pod exit (human decision, 2026-09-30; spec-changes §12, decision 5; D8). The pod-exit drain belongs to remediation-plan steps R16 and R17. A finding that asks for a pod-exit frame, an edge out of `READY` in §15.4.2, or a `DrainRuntimeForPodExit` re-opens that decision.
- The coordinator hold timeout ends the sidecar runtime through the pod-scope teardown (D10, CODE-4). It is a pod-scope event, so it is not a per-session close and does not reopen D2 or D3. Retirement after it uses the existing `runtime_not_live`, failed-start, and orphan-claim paths. Adapter exit at the timeout and a gateway `AdapterTerminating` consumer are pre-existing gaps (summary, Defects in the shipped tree).
- Pinned idle inventory is the interim rule (human decision, 2026-09-30, option D; D14); SPEC-8(c) states it. A finding that asks for part of the follow-up's scope (spec-changes §9.1, **Pinned idle bound and idle timeout**) re-opens the decision.
- The new retire condition has one statement, the §5.2 Pod retirement policy item "Runtime not live" (D15). Every other site names the item by its label, and TEST-16 fails on a restatement.
- Checklist step lines are pointers: deliverable ids, every id written out, plus the owning deliverables' heading names (reconcile.checklist.1).
- Decisions 1 to 5 and redesign decisions A to C are adjudicated (2026-09-30; spec-changes §12). A finding that argues for a rejected alternative re-opens a human decision.

### Traps

- Commits 8cdd5d6d through ccaeb30d (reverted by 39e08bb4) re-opened each facet one round at a time. Check any new finding against D3, D4, D8, and D9 before editing.
- The embedded runtime's process is the adapter's, which every recycled pod keeps. The admission rule applies to an embedded pool as well (spec-changes §12, decision 2).
- `{tenant_id} → unassigned` has no writer. The lifelong pin is what D6 stands on.
- The adapter never reads the acknowledgment or the cross-tenant setting. A finding asking for a wire field for either re-opens D2.
- `tests/tier11_docs/vm_restart_reprovision_consistency_test.go` pins phrases in the §5.2 recycle-lifecycle sentence and the §6.2 projection sentence. Append to those sentences; do not reword them.
- WATCHOUT: the `lenny.dev/runtime-recreatable` pod label and the `no_successor_runtime` retire (reconciler stamp, gateway parse, `Inputs.RuntimeRecreatable`) are deleted. They retired every sidecar recycling pod at occupancy zero, and the 2026-09-30 human decision keeps the runtime process instead. Decision 2 took Alternative B, so the label does not return.
- WATCHOUT: the successor-runtime mechanisms (a restartable runtime container, an adapter-side spawn generalising `SpawnPath`, and a platform supervisor as the runtime container's PID 1) are deleted from §9. A kept process needs no successor, so a finding that proposes one re-derives a rejected design.
- WATCHOUT: an adapter-side conditional close at the last slot lost to the unconditional rule (D2). On the concurrent path the recycle `Shutdown` arrives after the last slot's `Close` (`pkg/adapter/session.go`, `answerShutdown`), so the adapter cannot see the disposition when it would close.
- WATCHOUT: rejecting `in-place` with `allowCrossTenantReuse` at pool validation lost to the tenant-change drain (D6) by human decision 4. Do not restage the validation rejection.
- WATCHOUT: clearing the reader-stopped flag on a new accept, and any runtime redial, are deleted. No second accept exists once the connection is kept, and redial was a facet of the reverted attempt (commit 68fb2a01). `ended` is sticky (D4).
- WATCHOUT: positive `RuntimeLive` input on `Decide` lost to retire polarity (D13), because every existing reuse literal (`podscrub_test.go` `base()`, the tier-4 recycle literal) would flip to retire. Only the wire field `runtime_live` is positive.
- WATCHOUT: placing the new retire branch before the session-count branch, which the earlier label branch did by reusing the `VMRestart` reason-stability argument, lost to placing it after uptime (non-spec §4.3). Moving it earlier changes the counted reason of every existing `maxSessionsPerPod: 1` pool.
- WATCHOUT: the tier-7a concurrent retire-once case (old TEST-8) is deleted. It exercised `SlotClaimer.ReleaseSlot`, which this proposal does not change; TEST-15 extends the existing `vm_restart_recycle_disposition` scenario instead.
- WATCHOUT: a separate §6.2 `claimed ──→ draining` edge lost to clauses on the one recycle edge (SPEC-9(c)), because `vm-restart` is already a clause inside that edge.
- WATCHOUT: the §16.1 retirement-counter row edit (old SPEC-8) is dropped and `spec/16` left the files-touched list. The row's omission of the non-counting reasons predates this proposal and is filed separately (§9.1).
- WATCHOUT: the session-mode and no-claim conditions of the pinned-idle predicate, and the uncached confirmation of a missing claim, are load-bearing (non-spec §4.5, CODE-10); dropping any of them drains pods that are serving or being acquired.
- WATCHOUT: a checklist range such as "SPEC-9 through SPEC-16" is read by implement-proposal-build.js:1414 as two ids and narrows the spec/ lease. Write every id out (reconcile.checklist.1).
- WATCHOUT: appending the pinned drains to `Plan.Drain` lost to a separate `DrainPinned` list (redesign1.apply.2). Every consumer subtracts `len(Drain)` from counts that exclude pinned pods, so a shared list drives `status.readyCount` and `status.warmCount` negative (non-spec §4.5).
- WATCHOUT: reading an absent label or `unassigned` as unpinned lost to the SPEC-8(c) predicate (redesign1.apply.2). `AdmitTenantPin` refuses `unassigned` to every tenant, so the planner counts it as pinned; only a missing Pod reads as unpinned.
- WATCHOUT: restating the new retire condition at a `vm-restart` carve-out lost to one statement in the §5.2 Pod retirement policy (D15), because the copies drifted apart each round. A site names the condition by its label.
- WATCHOUT: the "retirement audit record" for a `released` retire lost to the CODE-7 `Info` log (redesign1.apply.2), because no code writes such a record: `claimDispositionDriver.Retire` logs only a `failed` retire. Decision A (spec-changes §12) adopted the log.
- WATCHOUT: the recycle-boundary acknowledgment gate (`process_reuse_unacknowledged`, `Inputs.ProcessReuseUnacknowledged`, `PodRecyclePolicy.ProcessReuseUnacknowledged` set in `InspectForRecycle`, and the §5.2 item "Process reuse not acknowledged") lost to the CODE-11 admission rule (redesign1.apply.3) by human decision 2 (Alternative B). The gate admitted a pool whose every pod then retired after one session and needed a restatement at every `vm-restart` carve-out; the admission rule refuses the pool once, in `validateRecyclePolicy`.
- WATCHOUT: sending the `terminate` frame once at pod exit (`Server.DrainRuntimeForPodExit`, its `cmd/lenny-adapter` SIGTERM call, TEST-17, and a §15.4.2 `READY` → `DRAINING` edge) lost to no send on any path (redesign1.apply.3) by human decision 5. The frame means process exit and the kubelet's SIGTERM races it; the pod-exit drain belongs to remediation steps R16 and R17.
- WATCHOUT: ending the sidecar runtime at the coordinator hold timeout through the last member's per-session `Close` in `terminateHeldSession` lost to the pod-scope teardown that `onHoldTimeout` calls (CODE-4, redesign1.apply.3). Under D3 that `Close` ends nothing, so the runtime would keep a terminated session's credentials with no coordinator.
- WATCHOUT: running the hold-timeout teardown after `deregisterStartedSessions` lost to running it before pass 1 and after `hold.mu` is released (redesign1.apply.3). The hold is cleared first, so a `Start` admitted after the clear must see `ended`, or it confirms against a registry entry pass 1 never collected.

### Per-session-process blast radius

Rule. A sentence that assumes a per-session runtime process or an adapter signal path to the runtime is staged when (a) it is the home of a fact this proposal decides (who starts the runtime, §4.7.9 step 7; the runtime's lifetime, §4.7.10), (b) this proposal changes its truth (what a session end, interrupt, missed heartbeat, or occupancy zero does to the runtime; when `terminate` is sent), or (c) it restates a staged sentence in docs, a schema description, or a code comment. Otherwise it was already false before this proposal for a reason this proposal does not change, and it keeps its wording under a named spec-changes §9.1 class: P1 (the adapter signals the sidecar runtime or writes `shutdown`), P2 (the adapter reads the runtime's exit code or stderr), P3 (first-session start-up ordering), or OD3 (spec-changes §12, decision 3). T means true after this proposal. Staged text never restates a P-class claim. This table supersedes the per-round WATCHOUT, OPEN, and UNVERIFIED entries on these sites. File a site not in this table only after classifying it by this rule.

Ground truth for classes P1 and P2 is the summary's defect entry on adapter signals (summary.md, Defects in the shipped tree). `terminate` has one production writer in the shipped tree (pkg/adapter/session.go:512-520, reached only from `tearDownReclaimedSlot`) and none after this proposal (D8).

### Open

- **Sidecar preConnect** (spec.1.fix-G1.1, spec.1.fix-design-G1.1): whether §6.1 restricts `preConnect` to the embedded model, given that nothing starts a sidecar SDK process after SPEC-1 and SPEC-2.
- **§4.6.1 "genuinely exhausted"** (spec.1.fix-G4.1, spec.1.fix-design-G4.1): after the pin read, spec/04:440 means "no idle pod the pin admits".
- **pod_reconciler drain-count comment** (spec.1.fix-G4.1, spec.1.fix-design-G4.1): pkg/controller/warmpool/pod_reconciler.go:163 and :603 say a drain-requested pod "was already counted by whichever gateway path stamped the annotation", which the non-counting tenant-change stamp falsifies; no CODE-9 bullet names it.
- **Cross-tenant drain of every refused pinned pod** (spec.4.review-performance.1): whether an acquisition drains at most one pinned pod.
- **Fallback admit inside the row-locked transaction** (spec.1.review-performance.1, spec.2.review-performance.1): AdmitTenantPin runs an apiserver read, and on an `allowCrossTenantReuse` pool a drain patch, under `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` (non-spec §4.4).
- **CH-MSGSOCK Preconditions are first-session only** (spec.4.fix-design-G1.1): spec/28:555-560 is kept verbatim under §9.1 and does not say so.
- **TEST-11 and the zero pinned-idle bound** (reconcile.mechanisms.1): `task-mode-echo-pool` is an admin-API pool, so its pinned-idle bound is zero until demand is observed (D14), and TEST-11's session B reaches session A's pod only if it binds within the fixture's `gateway.claimHoldTTLSeconds` of session A's end. No pass has checked the case's timing against the hold.

### Deferred

- DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md] (spec.1.review-client-surface.1, spec.2.review-client-surface.1): `pkg/gateway/externalapi/openapi/openapi.json` describes `allowCrossTenantReuse` (`:393`) as "§5.2 cross-tenant sequential reuse", and the Go field comment at `pkg/gateway/runtime/runtimestore/runtimestore.go:1078-1081` says the same. After SPEC-8(f), the cross-tenant field drains a pod that a different tenant's session would reach. DOC-4 corrects only `docs/reference/configuration.md`, and no deliverable corrects the OpenAPI description or the Go comment. CODE-11 corrects the `acknowledgeProcessLevelIsolation` OpenAPI description, and CODE-9 corrects its Go comment (`runtimestore.go:1024-1028`).
- DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md] (spec.4.review-docs-alignment.1): `docs/reference/execution-modes.md:59` ("`podReuse` and `residualStateWarning` are `true` whenever the pod serves more than one session over its lifetime") and `docs/client-guide/session-lifecycle.md:400` ("the session runs on a pod that serves more than one session over its lifetime") describe the fields by pod behaviour. The fields are computed from pool configuration and report `true` on an unacknowledged recycling pool whose pods retire after one session. DOC-5 names `session-lifecycle.md:400` only to append the kept-process clause, and DOC-2 does not name `execution-modes.md:59`.

## Ledger

### [redesign1.apply.1]

- DECISION: redesign 1 replaced the proposal's premise and every mechanism built on it — BECAUSE the human decisions of 2026-09-30 keep the runtime process across occupancy zero on recycling pools with `maxSessionsPerPod > 1` — ALTERNATIVES: the label-keyed boundary retire of every sidecar recycling pod, deleted.
- FACT: areas redesigned: runtime lifetime across the recycle boundary (D1, D3, D10, CODE-1, CODE-4), boundary liveness (D4, CODE-5, the `runtime_live` wire field), the process-reuse acknowledgment gate (D5, CODE-6, CODE-7), the tenant pin on every idle acquisition including the Postgres fallback claim and pinned-idle inventory (D6, CODE-8, CODE-10), the terminate frame at pod exit (D8, CODE-2), and the runtime generation (D9, CODE-3).
- FACT: deleted with the old design: the label and `no_successor_runtime` (old CODE-1 to CODE-3, TEST-1 to TEST-3), old SPEC-1 to SPEC-8 and D1 to D7, §9.1 to §9.5, the embedded recycling fixture and its tier-5 case (old FIXTURE-1, TEST-6), the tier-7a retire-once case (old TEST-8), the `upgrades.md` rollout note, and `spec/16` from files touched. The design also deletes `tests/tier7a_load_local/shutdown_drain_gate_race_test.go` and proposal 0078's TEST-1 and TEST-9.
- OPEN: five open decisions recorded in spec changes §12, each with a default: pod-global surfaces after a kept process's first session (fail closed), the scope of the acknowledgment gate (uniform), the runtime contract for sequential sessions (sibling SDK proposal), the relationship to proposal 0078 (land 0078 first), and the terminate frame (send once at pod exit).
- FACT: every anchor in redesign 1 matched and all edits E1 to E22 applied. The post-apply sweep removed history from spec changes §9.1, open decision 2's Alternative A, the summary's first fixed decision, and problem statement §1.7.
- UNVERIFIED: the redesign's file:line citations were carried in as written and were not re-checked against the tree by the applier; the next review round should verify them.


### [spec.1.fix-G1.1]

- DECISION: SPEC-8(d)'s positive-reuse sentence ("serves its tenant's later sessions up to maxSessionsPerPod") is deleted rather than qualified with vm-restart — BECAUSE SPEC-5(b) and the Pod retirement policy carry the reuse outcome; a qualifier would keep a second copy of the reuse predicate.

### [spec.1.fix-G2.1]

- DECISION: the no-signal rule has one home, SPEC-2's §4.7.10 "Runtime process lifetime" sentence (no session teardown, interrupt, heartbeat escalation, or occupancy-zero boundary closes the connection or signals the process). §15.4.3 (SPEC-11(c)), §28.5.3/§28.8 (SPEC-14), §29.4 step 6 (SPEC-12(e)), and §11.4 (SPEC-13) point to it — BECAUSE D3 makes `SocketRuntimeProcess.Interrupt` a no-op, and the spec's SIGTERM promises for interrupt and heartbeat escalation had no implementation left — ALTERNATIVES: an Interrupt that closes the shared connection at the last live slot (re-opens D2 and breaks sibling slots under §5.2 Failure isolation); a sticky hung flag read by `ServesNextSession` (a new mechanism for a marginal case that would retire a pod after one slow ack).
- DECISION: the pointer homes are the §15.4.3 `Interrupt` row for interrupt degradation (cited by SPEC-12(e) and SPEC-14(c)/(d)) and the §28.5.3 `CH-MSGSOCK` **Timing.** bullet for heartbeat escalation (cited by SPEC-14(b)/(e) and SPEC-11(c)'s Basic-list bullet).
- WATCHOUT: do not restate pod-exit SIGTERM/SIGKILL mechanics in staged text. The adapter shares no process namespace with the runtime container, and the kubelet SIGKILLs at `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` (default 120s), so "SIGTERM to agent, wait up to 10s, then SIGKILL" (old SPEC-13) and "the adapter sends SIGTERM on timeout" (old SPEC-12(b)) described signals that no component sends. SPEC-2's "the pod's termination ends the process" is the only statement — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go (preStop only on the adapter container), pkg/adapter/session.go `drainViaLifecycle` (writes the frame only).
- WATCHOUT: §29.9 step 4b (SPEC-12(f)) must not say the frame follows the preStop hook. `runPreStop` SIGTERMs the adapter at the start of preStop, and CODE-4 sends the frame from that SIGTERM goroutine, so the 4a/4b order stays unfixed — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-adapter/prestop.go `runPreStop`.
- FACT: `onHeartbeatHung` calls `rt.Interrupt` and the Attach loop then ends the stream, so "the adapter ends that session's stream" in SPEC-14(a) is the post-D3 behaviour — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/heartbeat.go `onHeartbeatHung`.
- FACT: `DrainRuntimeForPodExit` became parameterless (CODE-2, CODE-4) because the deleted SPEC-13 parenthetical was the source of CODE-4's "§11.4 10-second grace"; it passes `defaultSocketShutdownGrace` (10s, pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go) in milliseconds.
- MISTAKE: redesign1.apply.1 wrote the pod-exit shutdown sequence at two staged sites (SPEC-12(b), SPEC-13) with different mechanisms, and left §29.9 step 4b `unstated`, §29.4 step 12's "adapter closes the session runtime", and the §15.4.3 per-session `shutdown`/SIGTERM statements unstaged. It cost this round five findings.

### [spec.1.fix-G4.1]

- DECISION: the tenant-change drain-request is attributed by pointer at §5.2 on both §6.2 drain-request edges (SPEC-9(e)) and by deleting ` when a pod crosses the unhealthy threshold` from the §4.6.3 RBAC paragraph (SPEC-4(d)) — BECAUSE §5.2 is the one home of drain-request triggers and the RBAC parenthetical already cites §5.2 — ALTERNATIVES: enumerating both triggers on each edge (third copy of §5.2's list); a separate claimed→draining edge (lost earlier, see Traps).
- FACT: the only tier-11 pin near the §6.2 idle edge is `level-triggers the drain from the pod CreationTimestamp` (tests/tier11_docs/concurrent_slot_lifecycle_doc_reconciliation_test.go TestUptimeDrainMechanismAgrees_F5231); `regardless of session activity` is pinned in §5.2 and state-machines.md only. SPEC-9(e) keeps both phrases. — EVIDENCE: that test file, lines 285-345.
- DECISION: a pin refusal on the Postgres fallback passes over the row to the next idle candidate inside ClaimIdle's one transaction (SPEC-8(c) clause; CODE-8 loop excluding refused pod_ids; refused rows never updated) — BECAUSE claimSelectSQL is `ORDER BY updated_at LIMIT 1 FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` and the mirror writes tenant_id empty for idle rows, so SQL cannot filter the pin — ALTERNATIVES: tenant_id projection into idle mirror rows (contradicts §12 "NULL when idle"), batch LIMIT n (locks rows it never needs).
- WATCHOUT: the withdrawn CODE-8 IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE (restore the row's prior state after a refused commit, then retry) lost because restoring updated_at makes `ORDER BY updated_at LIMIT 1` reselect the same refused row, so a retry never advances. Do not restage it. — EVIDENCE: pkg/agentpodstate/pgstore/pgstore.go claimSelectSQL.
- DECISION: pinned idle pods count toward maxWarm but not minWarm, and the WarmPoolController drains pinned idle pods beyond maxWarm minus minWarm (SPEC-8(c); CODE-10 static cap in plan.Compute, sorted name order) — BECAUSE §17 pods_floor = sum(pool.maxWarm) already sizes the quota and §4.6.1 "between minWarm and maxWarm" stays true — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's dynamic longest-idle drain (plan.Pod has no idle-since input; pool may sit above ceiling), pinned-idle TTL or new pool field (second mechanism for what maxWarm bounds), counting pinned toward the target (reopens the starvation D6 closes).
- WATCHOUT: on a pool with minWarm == maxWarm the pinned cap is 0, so every pinned pod that returns to idle is drained on the next reconcile and reuse beyond the reserved hold does not happen there. This is the design's accepted consequence; a finding that reuse "does not work" on such a pool should be checked against it before a new mechanism is proposed. The fixture (FIXTURE-1) runs minWarm = maxWarm and TEST-11 claims its second session inside the reserved hold. — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/plan/plan.go warmTarget.
- FACT: plan.Compute target = minWarm clamped to [0, maxWarm]; the excess drain sorts idle names and takes the first `excess`; nothing today drains above maxWarm except through the warm count. — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/plan/plan.go Compute, warmTarget.
- OPEN: the §4.6.1 fallback paragraph still says a fallback miss means "the warm pool is genuinely exhausted" (spec/04 **Fallback claim path via Postgres:**); after the pin read it means no idle pod the pin admits. Separate finding per the design; nothing staged.
- OPEN: the §6.2 `idle ──→ draining` edge lists neither the planner's excess drain nor the new pinned-cap drain; pre-existing gap, separate finding per the design.
- OPEN: pkg/controller/warmpool/pod_reconciler.go comment "a drain-requested pod was already counted by whichever gateway path stamped the annotation" is false for the non-counting tenant-change stamp (D7); a CODE-9 comment finding for a later non-spec round.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]

- DECISION: SDK-warm vs the kept runtime process is closed by one definitional sentence in SPEC-2 (the §6.1 SDK process that `DemoteSDK` tears down and the re-warm restarts is distinct from the runtime process, and neither ends it) plus deleting ", with its SDK connection," from SPEC-9(a) — BECAUSE in the embedded model SPEC-2 already makes the runtime process the adapter process, which DemoteSDK never restarts, so the two terms must differ; the distinction keeps D1, summary "What changes" bullet 1, SPEC-1, and SPEC-12(a) step 25 true unchanged — ALTERNATIVES: an "except where §6.1 demotion/re-warm restarts" carve-out (rejected: makes the lifetime conditional, falsifies D1 and the Settled line, and implies the SDK process is the runtime process); an open decision (rejected: no conflict with the 2026-09-30 decisions once the terms are separated).
- FACT: the only SDKWarmRuntime implementation is embedded (`SDKWarmInProcessRuntime`, pkg/adapter/embedded_sdkwarm.go); its "SDK" is the in-process loop, DemoteSDK closes the loop and leaves the adapter process running. `SocketRuntimeProcess` does not implement SDKWarmRuntime, so a sidecar adapter answers PreConnect/ConfigureWorkspace/DemoteSDK as a pod-warm adapter — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/sdkwarm.go:140, :215, :316.
- OPEN: the spec never restricts `preConnect` to the embedded model, yet after SPEC-1 the adapter cannot start or restart anything in a sidecar runtime container, so sidecar SDK-warm has no mechanism. Pre-existing in the tree; a later round or a human should decide whether §6.1 states it.
- DECISION: §29.2 step 29 is replaced in place by a traced `runtime` → `adapter`, `CH-MSGSOCK`, `intra-pod` step that names step 25 as its point — BECAUSE §29.1 says an `unstated` step keeps its number and position, and step 30 cites "steps 24 through 29" — ALTERNATIVES: fold into step 25 and delete step 29 (rejected: renumbering; CommonMark would render "30." as 29).
- DECISION: SPEC-2 drops "under the pod's `RestartPolicy: Never`" rather than staging a §4.7.11 edit — BECAUSE the clause does no work for the lifetime rule, and deleting it leaves §4.7.11 item 4 and spec/10 (the adapter mTLS handshake paragraph), which both name `CrashLoopBackOff`, exactly as wrong as today.
- UNVERIFIED: §4.7.11 item 4 and spec/10's adapter handshake-failure sentence say the pod enters `CrashLoopBackOff`, while `basePod` renders `RestartPolicy: Never` (pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go:980). This is a pre-existing spec-versus-tree defect, separate from 0079.
- WATCHOUT: "What the scrub reaches" (SPEC-6(b)) is the single home for which state survives the scrub in each model. D5, D11, SPEC-6(c), and SPEC-8(d) cite it. A later finding asking any of them to list memory, descriptors, children, or environment re-opens this group.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]

- DECISION: the one home for "no per-session event ends or signals the kept runtime" is SPEC-2's §4.7.10 **Runtime process lifetime** sentence, extended to name interrupt and heartbeat escalation. §15.4.3 (SPEC-11(c)), §28.5.3 (new SPEC-14), §29.4 step 6 (SPEC-12(e)), and §11.4 (SPEC-13) cite it rather than restate it. BECAUSE D3 makes `SocketRuntimeProcess.Interrupt` a no-op, so every spec "SIGTERM on interrupt/hung heartbeat" statement loses its implementation. ALTERNATIVES: amend D3 so that Interrupt closes the connection on the sole session. Rejected because it re-derives the adapter-side conditional close that D2 rules out, and on a concurrent pod it would EOF every sibling (§5.2 Failure isolation, "Other slots continue unaffected").
- DECISION: the heartbeat-hung sidecar runtime stays on the pod, and no hung flag feeds `ServesNextSession`. The accepted outcome is stated as one clause on the ReportPodScrub row (SPEC-3(c)). BECAUSE a sequential pod already retires on the failed session and a concurrent pod on the slot-retry trigger, so a new flag adds a mechanism for marginal gain and would retire a pod after one slow ack.
- DECISION: the pod-exit terminate frame is traced once, at §29.9 step 4b (SPEC-12(f)), and §29.4 step 13 points to it. The Basic/Standard "SIGTERM-based" sentence and "adapter sends SIGTERM on timeout" are deleted from staged text rather than reworded to name the kubelet.
- FACT: at pod deletion the kubelet SIGTERMs the runtime container immediately, because it has no preStop hook (`pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go`, runtime container, no `Lifecycle`). The adapter's own preStop SIGTERMs the adapter at the START of the preStop window (`cmd/lenny-adapter/prestop.go` `runPreStop` → `syscall.Kill(1, SIGTERM)`), and the SIGTERM goroutine in `cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go` then calls `lifecycle.Close()` before `srv.GracefulStop()`. So CODE-4's frame goes out at the start of preStop, concurrent with or before the eviction checkpoint (§29.9 step 4a), and to a runtime the kubelet is already terminating. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-adapter/prestop.go:67-100, cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:413-426
- OPEN: open decision 5 (the terminate frame at pod exit) should record that the frame reaches a Full-level runtime concurrently with, or after, the kubelet's SIGTERM to the runtime container. That fact weighs toward its Alternative (delete `drainViaLifecycle`). This is for the decisions pass; the design leaves it unstaged.
- WATCHOUT: before the proposal, `lifecycle.Close()` at the adapter's SIGTERM already closes CH-RUNTIMEOPS before the preStop eviction checkpoint could send `checkpoint_request` to a Full-level runtime. This is a pre-existing defect, separate from 0079. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:424
- WATCHOUT: `pkg/adapter` sends no `shutdown` frame anywhere, and the adapter cannot SIGTERM a sidecar runtime container. The spec's pre-existing "adapter sends SIGTERM ... then SIGKILL" statements (§28.5.3 `shutdown` :624, the Timing `shutdown` sentence, the `terminate` row :1082, :1126, and the §15.4.3 Graceful-drain row and bullet) are therefore independently wrong and were left as separate findings. Do not fold them into this proposal without a finding.
- DECISION: SPEC-13 deletes the 10s/SIGKILL parenthetical, so CODE-4's "§11.4 10-second grace" loses its source. `DrainRuntimeForPodExit` becomes parameterless and uses `defaultSocketShutdownGrace` internally, because `cmd/lenny-adapter` cannot name that unexported constant. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:473
- OPEN (non-spec loop): after SPEC-14, the code text that says the heartbeat escalation sends SIGTERM contradicts the spec: `pkg/adapter/heartbeat.go` `onHeartbeatHung` doc and log line, `pkg/adapter/attach.go` hung-case comment and the error string "sent SIGTERM (§28.5.3)", `cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go` `--heartbeat-ack-timeout-seconds` help and the :299 comment, and the `pkg/adapter/lifecycle.go` `Interrupt` doc ("sends SIGTERM"). D3 made these false; CODE-9 should absorb them.
- DEFERRED [0079_...implementation-checklist.md]: S3 names "SPEC-9, SPEC-10, SPEC-11, SPEC-12, SPEC-13" and "§29.2 step 25 and §29.4 steps 12, 13, and 17". After this round it must also name SPEC-14 (§28.5.3), SPEC-11(c) (§15.4.3), and §29.4 step 6 and §29.9 step 4b (SPEC-12(e), (f)). S11 says "DOC-1 through DOC-5", which must become DOC-1 through DOC-6.

D1 made it false and it is in no edit list. The §28.8 `CH-MSGSOCK` row's "is treated as hung and is sent SIGTERM" was not among the finding's sites; this design brings it into SPEC-14(e). ### [spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]

- DECISION: the §6.1 recycle opt-in is fixed by naming both acknowledgments in its existing parenthetical (SPEC-9(g)), which already ends in a §5.2 pointer — BECAUSE it is the shortest true statement; with only `acknowledgeBestEffortScrub` a recycling pod retires at its first boundary, so the relaxation needs both — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's appended "when the pool also sets ..." clause (longer, same effect).
- DECISION: the credential guarantees are qualified by pointers to SPEC-8(d)'s label "Deployer acknowledgment (runtime process kept across sessions)", never by restating what the kept process carries — BECAUSE SPEC-8(d) is the one home of that rule — ALTERNATIVES: the security reviewer's wording "where a runtime process and the processes it started persist into later sessions" restates SPEC-6(b)/SPEC-8(d).
- DECISION: §13.1's "sequential pod reuse under `recycle.enabled: true` keeps that property" is replaced by the guidance "and on a recycling pool leave `acknowledgeProcessLevelIsolation` unset", not qualified with "on a pool without the acknowledgment" — BECAUSE after D5 a recycling pool without the acknowledgment reuses no pod, so that qualifier makes the clause vacuous.
- FACT: the §13.1 exposure is real. A subprocess session N's runtime started survives the boundary in the sidecar model, runs as the agent UID with `lenny-cred-readers` fsGroup membership, and can read `/run/lenny/slots/{N+1}/credentials.json`. The per-slot co-tenancy §13.1 grants only on `maxConcurrentSessions > 1` pools therefore also applies to acknowledged sequential recycling pools. The lease count (one at a time) stays true. — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:28, :30; spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:26
- FACT: no test pins "single-session trust boundary", "with or without recycling" (§13.1), "keeps that property", "does not retain credentials", or "recycled across whole sessions"; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:351 "with or without recycling" is about checkpoint retention and is not a site.
- WATCHOUT: G1 may rename or restructure SPEC-8(d)'s bold label. The pointers SPEC-9(g)/(h) and SPEC-14 add must follow whatever label G1 settles.
- DEFERRED [0079_...implementation-checklist.md]: S3 lists "SPEC-9, SPEC-10, SPEC-11, SPEC-12, SPEC-13" and "The §6.1 row ..."; after this fix it must also list SPEC-14 and name the §6.1 recycle opt-in and credential-lease sentences and the §13.1 credential boundary.


### [spec.1.fix-design-G4.1]

- DECISION: pinned idle pods count toward `maxWarm` but not the `minWarm` target, and the WarmPoolController drains pinned idle pods beyond `maxWarm` minus the target (one rule, in SPEC-8(c)) — BECAUSE it bounds pool size by the ceiling the §17 quota floor already sums (`pods_floor = sum(pool.maxWarm)`, spec/17_deployment-topology.md:1105) and keeps §4.6.1 "between minWarm and maxWarm" true — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's dynamic form (drain the longest-idle pinned pod only when unpinned inventory is short at the ceiling) equals the static cap in steady state, leaves the ceiling exceeded while unpinned sits at target, and needs an idle-since input `plan.Pod` lacks; counting pinned idle toward the target (delete CODE-10) starves other tenants behind pod warm-up; draining every pod an other-tenant scan passes over (generalising the cross-tenant drain) mass-evicts under multi-tenant load and re-opens D6's human decision; retiring at reserved-hold expiry removes the §4.6.1 pinned-idle inventory the spec already defines.
- WATCHOUT: pools written through the pool store render `MinWarm = MaxWarm = warmCount` (pkg/controller/poolscaling/poolstoresource.go:107-108), so the cap is 0 on every admin-API pool and a pinned pod reuses its runtime only inside the reserved hold unless the deployer raises `maxWarm` (`PUT /v1/admin/pools/{name}/warm-count`). This is the intended trade-off, not a bug; TEST-11 claims session B during the hold (tests/tier5_e2e_kind/execution_modes_test.go `waitClaimPhase(... "reserved" ...)`) and is unaffected. — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/poolscaling/poolstoresource.go:107-108
- DECISION: a pin refusal on the fallback claim moves to the next idle row inside `ClaimIdle`'s one transaction (re-run the locked SELECT excluding refused pod_ids) — BECAUSE it keeps the change inside the store and needs no spec schema change — ALTERNATIVES: projecting the pin into `agent_pod_state.tenant_id` for idle rows and filtering in SQL is O(1) but contradicts §12's schema comment ("NULL when idle; set on claim", spec/12_storage-architecture.md:488) and the mirror sync overwrites tenant_id with empty today (pkg/controller/warmpool/controller.go:453-455, pgstore.go upsertSQL), so it needs §12 and §4.2 edits; `SELECT ... LIMIT n` locking many rows at once holds locks on rows never needed.
- WATCHOUT: CODE-8's former IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE (restore the row after a refusal instead of a callback) is unsafe once refusal must continue: restoring `updated_at` makes the next `ORDER BY updated_at LIMIT 1` pick the same refused row. Do not reintroduce it. — EVIDENCE: pkg/agentpodstate/pgstore/pgstore.go:222-228
- WATCHOUT: refused rows stay row-locked until the fallback transaction ends, so the pinned tenant's own concurrent fallback skips its pod for that window (SKIP LOCKED). Accepted: the window is one transaction and the number of refusals is bounded by the pinned-idle cap. — EVIDENCE: pkg/agentpodstate/pgstore/pgstore.go:222-228
- DECISION: finding 15 closes by deleting the trigger qualifier from the §4.6.3 RBAC sentence and replacing both §6.2 drain-request labels with `— see §5.2` — BECAUSE the triggers' home is §5.2 (whole-pod replacement trigger, spec/05:577; tenant-change drain, SPEC-8(c)); SPEC-9(e)'s long restatement of the tenant-change rule in the ASCII block shrinks to the pointer.
- FACT: the WarmPoolController's drain-request consumer is not idle-gated, so a tenant-change stamp can drain a claimed pod. — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/pod_reconciler.go reconcileDrainRequest, `patchSandboxDraining(ctx, r.Client, key, false)`
- FACT: `ClaimIdle` has a real-store conformance test at tests/tier2_component/stores/agentpodstatestore_test.go `TestAgentPodStateClaimIdle`; the skip-refused loop is pinned there, not only through `fakeMirror`.
- OPEN: `reconcileDrainRequest`'s doc comment (pod_reconciler.go) says "a drain-requested pod was already counted by whichever gateway path stamped the annotation"; the tenant-change stamp counts nothing (D7), so the comment goes false after this proposal. Non-spec CODE-9 should own it; not filed by G4.
- OPEN: §6.2's `idle ──→ draining` edge has never listed the planner's excess drain (plan.go `plan.Drain`), and the new pinned-cap drain falls in the same gap. Pre-existing; a later round may file it.
- OPEN: §4.6.1 fallback sentence "If the Postgres fallback also finds no idle pods (the warm pool is genuinely exhausted)" (spec/04_system-components.md:440) reads loosely once the fallback reads the pin; "no idle pod the pin admits" would be exact. Separate finding.
- DEFERRED [0079_fix_name-who-starts-the-next-sessions-runtime-on-a-recycled-pod.implementation-checklist.md]: S8's "pinned idle pods excluded from the unpinned warm target" omits the ceiling drain CODE-10 now carries; true instead: S8 should name CODE-10 without describing it (pointer rule), or say "pinned idle accounting and ceiling drain".


### [spec.1.fix-design-G5.1]
DECISION: SPEC-11(a) §15.4 is cut to a pointer at SPEC-5(b)'s §5.2 **Recycling and integration levels** paragraph; the sessionId-keying and runtime-exit sentences are deleted from §15.4 — BECAUSE the recycling-vs-integration-level contract has one home (§5.2) and the defect this proposal fixes was exactly §15.4 and §5.2 both stating it — ALTERNATIVES: syncing the two copies (adds maxSessionsPerPod to §15.4) rejected as a second home.
DECISION: the §5.1 **Setup Commands and Policy** "Per-task setup belongs in the runtime's initialization." sentence is staged inside SPEC-5(a) as a pointer-bearing replacement, and SPEC-5's heading and the summary's section list gain §5.1 — BECAUSE SPEC-5(a) already owns the per-session-setup rule.
DECISION: SPEC-7(a) also rewrites the §5.2 **Uptime limit** item's "if omitted, only `maxSessionsPerPod` and `maxScrubFailures` govern retirement" to "if omitted, the other conditions in this list govern retirement" — BECAUSE naming the other conditions would re-enumerate a list that keeps growing.
FACT: none of the three replaced spec phrases (§15.4 fresh-process sentence, §5.1 per-task setup, §5.2 uptime "only ... govern") is pinned by a tier-11 test or quoted in docs/ or pkg/ — EVIDENCE: grep over tests docs pkg returns only spec/05:262, spec/05:505, and spec/15:1789.

### [spec.1.follow-up-fix.1]

- DECISION: SPEC-9(g) now replaces `The agent process is NOT started.` with `No session is bound, and no message for the next session has been delivered.`, and SPEC-2 names the SDK process as what Section 6.1 calls the agent process — BECAUSE the earlier substitute ("The runtime process has been given no session") redefined §6.1's "agent process" as the runtime process, so the unedited SDK-warm, demotion (spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:22, :38) and §15 DemoteSDK (spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1469) sentences read as ending the runtime process, and it was false on a recycled idle pod — ALTERNATIVES: the finding's "no message has been delivered" without "for the next session" (false on a recycled pod whose process received earlier sessions' messages); deleting SPEC-9(g) (leaves "agent process is NOT started" contradicting SPEC-1 on a sidecar pod, where the kubelet starts the runtime).
- DECISION: the pinned-idle cap stays `maxWarm` minus `minWarm`; SPEC-8(c) now states that on a pool whose `maxWarm` equals `minWarm` a recycled pod serves its tenant again only within the reserved hold and is drained when it returns to idle, and edge row "Pinned pod on a pool without `allowCrossTenantReuse`" now reads "Refused and left pinned for its tenant, or drained, as SPEC-8(c) states" — BECAUSE total inventory within `maxWarm` with an unpinned floor of `minWarm` forces pinned <= maxWarm - minWarm; any other cap either exceeds the §17 quota floor (`pods_floor = sum(pool.maxWarm)`) or starves other tenants — ALTERNATIVES: an open decision for a different cap (no cap satisfies both bounds, so it would reopen the fix-design-G4 decision without a candidate).
- FACT: every pool synced from the pool store renders `MaxWarm = warmCount` always and `MinWarm = warmCount` until demand-based minWarm converges, so the cap is 0 by default — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/poolscaling/poolstoresource.go:107-108, pkg/controller/poolscaling/controller.go:729-730.
- OPEN: whether losing kept-runtime reuse beyond the 10 s reserved hold on every default-configured pool is acceptable, or whether the pool store should render `maxWarm` above `minWarm` for recycling pools. The staged text now states the consequence; the trade-off is a human's call.
- DEFERRED [0079_fix_name-who-starts-the-next-sessions-runtime-on-a-recycled-pod.non-spec-changes.md]: CODE-10 appends the pinned-cap drain to `Plan.Drain`, but every consumer subtracts `len(Drain)` from `WarmCount` and `ReadyCount`, which never counted pinned pods (pkg/controller/warmpool/controller.go:329 `readyAfter`, `DerivePoolPhase` :354-356, `updateStatus` :695-697, `updateTemplateCondition` :784-786). With minWarm 2, maxWarm 3, two pinned idle pods and no unpinned pods, status.readyCount is written as -1. True instead: CODE-10 puts the pinned-cap drain in its own `Plan` field (such as `DrainPinned []string`), `Reconcile` drains both lists, and the count consumers keep subtracting only `len(Drain)`. TEST-18 needs a plan case and a controller case with pinned pods over the cap that assert `status.readyCount` and `status.warmCount` (its controller case uses maxWarm 4, which drains nothing). My edit did not falsify CODE-10, so the grant barred repairing it.
- DEFERRED [0079_fix_name-who-starts-the-next-sessions-runtime-on-a-recycled-pod.non-spec-changes.md]: §8.4 has no DOC-6 for the pages SPEC-11(c) and SPEC-14 falsify (missed heartbeat ends the session; interrupt sends no signal; Basic gets no `shutdown` at expiry): docs/reference/adapter-contract.md:202, docs/getting-started/concepts.md:133, docs/runtime-author-guide/integration-levels.md:28, :31, :59, docs/runtime-author-guide/echo-runtime.md:138, :234, docs/runtime-author-guide/local-development.md:346, docs/runtime-author-guide/lifecycle.md:275, :423, docs/tutorials/build-a-runtime.md:143, :464. §13 Documentation must list those files. Same remedy as spec.1.fix-G2's DEFERRED, with the site list re-verified.
- DEFERRED [0079_fix_name-who-starts-the-next-sessions-runtime-on-a-recycled-pod.non-spec-changes.md]: SPEC-5(a) retires "Per-task setup belongs in the runtime's initialization", but docs/runtime-author-guide/runtime-configuration.md:101 ("Per-session setup belongs in the runtime's own initialization rather than in setup commands.") and the `RunSetupRequest` comment in schemas/lenny-adapter.proto:869-870 still state it. True instead: DOC-2 rewrites the docs sentence and CODE-9 corrects the proto comment and regenerates pkg/proto/adapter/v1, each pointing per-session setup at the runtime's handling of the session's first message. Same remedy as spec.1.fix-G5's DEFERRED.
- CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-G1 DEFERRED glossary Pod-warm]: with SPEC-9(g) no longer redefining §6.1's "agent process", docs/reference/glossary.md:275 ("the agent process is NOT started") no longer contradicts §6.1, so DOC-2 owes no glossary edit for it.
- WATCHOUT: spec-changes.md still cites "open decision 1/2/3/5" in D5, D8, D9, and the edge table (rows for Full-level runtime, Go SDK, second session). The orchestrator bars referencing open decisions in staged files; a later pass should resolve those pointers. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:15, :21, :23, :87-89.

### [spec.1.review-kubernetes.1]

- FACT: the gateway's Kubernetes client is uncached (`client.New` at cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go:169), so AdmitTenantPin's Pod read on the fallback path does not read the degraded watch cache the fallback exists to bypass. A finding premised on a stale informer read is refuted. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go:169, pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/fallbackclaim.go:77-87
- FACT: on pod deletion the runtime container (no preStop) gets the kubelet SIGTERM at once; the adapter's preStop (`lenny-adapter prestop`) SIGTERMs the adapter itself, so the adapter's SIGTERM goroutine runs concurrently with, not before, the runtime's kubelet SIGTERM. Grace is terminationGracePeriodSeconds (default 120s), not 10s. `drainViaLifecycle` only writes the frame; nothing in the adapter signals the runtime container. — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go:600-620, :1296-1322, cmd/lenny-adapter/prestop.go:68-100, pkg/adapter/session.go:512-520
- FACT: the tenant-label webhook allows the WPC SA `{tenant}→unassigned` with no pool check, and forbids `unassigned→{tenant}`; SPEC-4(d)'s unconditional wording matches the code. — EVIDENCE: pkg/admission/label_immutability/label_immutability.go:197-222
- WATCHOUT: two WPC arms write Sandbox.status.phase for a claimed pod carrying drain-request: PodReconciler.reconcileDrainRequest patches draining (requireIdle=false) and OccupancyReconciler.ProjectOccupancyPhase re-projects `claimed` from a `bound` claim regardless of Current, so they can flap. Pre-existing for the concurrent unhealthy drain; this proposal makes it reachable on sequential cross-tenant pools (spec-changes §5 row "same-tenant session that binds before the drain lands"). Not filed (pre-existing mechanism). — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/pod_reconciler.go:609-640, pkg/controller/warmpool/occupancy.go:84-100,181-209
- DEFERRED [non-spec-changes]: CODE-10 makes a pinned idle pod neither warm, ready, nor a drain candidate, and the pin never clears, so a pool accumulates one or more idle pinned pods per tenant past maxWarm with no scale-down path except optional maxPodUptimeSeconds; with minWarm=maxWarm=0 (experiment conclusion) `status.readyCount` reaches 0 while pinned pods live, and spec/10_gateway-internals.md:1121 has the PSC delete the SandboxWarmPool then. What is true instead: CODE-10 must keep pinned idle pods subject to a bound (count them toward maxWarm or keep them drain candidates above the ceiling) and must not drop them from readyCount without a matching §10 edit. — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/plan/plan.go:104-150
- UNVERIFIED: SPEC-9(b) phrases the new retire as a projection of a `recycling` claim on scrub-report content no CRD carries; the actual path is the gateway's terminal `released` disposition (occupancy.go:103-106). Same phrasing as the existing vm-restart clause, so not filed; a correctness lens may want it reworded.


### [spec.1.review-mechanism.1]

- FACT: SDK-warm (preConnect) is the one place the spec already ends and re-creates the agent process inside a live pod: DemoteSDK "cleanly tear[s] down and restart[s] the agent process without restarting the pod", and a demoted recycling pod is "re-warmed at the next recycle boundary". Any unconditional "runtime process lives as long as the pod" sentence collides with it. — EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:38, :74; spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1469; pkg/adapter/embedded_sdkwarm.go:65-69 ("A real agent-SDK runtime SIGKILLs its SDK subprocess")
- WATCHOUT: SPEC-9(a) says the SDK connection is kept at the boundary while SPEC-6(b) says embedded step 1 ends the processes the runtime started and the same §6.1 row keeps "re-establishes SDK-warm state on the re-warm edge". Reconcile SPEC-2, SPEC-6(b), and SPEC-9(a) together or the next round finds the other half.
- FACT: agent pods render RestartPolicy: Never (pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go:980), and the spec never stated a restart policy until SPEC-2; §4.7.11 item 4 still says a failed SO_PEERCRED self-test puts the pod in CrashLoopBackOff, which cannot happen under Never. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:943
- FACT: the runtime-liveness retire predicate is written at about fifteen staged sites; only SPEC-3(c) and SPEC-7(a) carry the "or omits that fact" conjunct. Reduce to citations of the §5.2 "Runtime not live" item rather than re-synchronising copies.
- WATCHOUT: at pod termination the kubelet, not the adapter, SIGTERMs the sidecar runtime container and SIGKILLs after terminationGracePeriodSeconds (default 120s, spec/29 §29.9 preconditions). Staged text that says "the adapter sends SIGTERM on timeout" or "wait up to 10s, then SIGKILL" at pod termination assigns an action no actor performs as stated.
- FACT: §29.9 step 4b is an `unstated` marker about when the eviction terminate frame is sent; SPEC-3(a)/SPEC-12(b) state it (once, at pod termination), so 4b goes stale unless restaged. — EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:1414-1418
- OPEN: CODE-10 excludes pinned idle pods from the warm target and from drain candidates. With maxPodUptimeSeconds optional, a pinned idle pod whose tenant never returns is never retired, so the pool grows by one pod per distinct tenant. The lifelong pin predates this proposal (spec/05 §5.2 tenant pinning), so I did not file it in the spec loop; the non-spec loop should decide whether CODE-10 needs a bound.
- DEFERRED [non-spec-changes]: none derived.


### [spec.1.review-performance.1]

- FACT: the Postgres fallback claim selects exactly one row, `ORDER BY updated_at LIMIT 1 FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, and the mirror sync writes `tenant_id` empty for idle rows, so SQL cannot filter on the pin today — EVIDENCE: pkg/agentpodstate/pgstore/pgstore.go:222-228, pkg/controller/warmpool/controller.go:453-455
- WATCHOUT: non-spec CODE-8 and TEST-13 make a fallback claim whose single selected row is pinned to another tenant return ErrNoIdlePod, so the fallback fails whenever that row is pinned, even with unpinned idle rows present. Filed as a spec finding against SPEC-8(c) — EVIDENCE: 0079 non-spec-changes.md:78, :171
- FACT: the gateway has only get/patch on Pods (no list/watch), so AdmitTenantPin is a live apiserver GET per scanned candidate. The primary path already lists every Sandbox in the pool per claim, so the added GETs are small unless many pinned idle pods precede the first admissible one in list order — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:632, pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go:104-124
- DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md §4.4]: the in-transaction admit callback holds a Postgres row lock and connection across an apiserver GET (and, on allowCrossTenantReuse pools, a drain PATCH). The fallback only runs while the apiserver is degraded, when §4.6.1 expects 5-15s of added latency. The IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE alternative, which restores the row after a refusal, avoids this.
- FACT: the WarmPoolController has a Pod informer (pod_reconciler.go:758), so CODE-10's per-idle-Sandbox Pod read comes from the cache and adds no apiserver load — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/pod_reconciler.go:740-758
- FACT: sessions_served is a Postgres counter, and RecordPodScrub fails closed on an increment error, so the maxSessionsPerPod bound on a kept process survives a Redis reset — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/leasecontrol/scrubreport_server.go:461-467
- WATCHOUT: CODE-10 excludes pinned idle pods from WarmCount and from Drain, and maxPodUptimeSeconds is optional, so nothing bounds pinned idle inventory by maxWarm or the §17.1 ResourceQuota floor. Filed as a spec finding (bound it in SPEC-8(c)) — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/plan/plan.go:104-141, spec/17_deployment-topology.md:105


### [spec.1.review-reliability.1]

- FACT: the runtime container carries no preStop hook; only the adapter container (sidecar) and the embedded runtime container do. At pod deletion the kubelet SIGTERMs the runtime container immediately and SIGKILLs it at terminationGracePeriodSeconds (default 120s), so the adapter's pod-exit terminate frame arrives after the runtime already has SIGTERM. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go:606 (adapter Lifecycle), :608-615 (runtime container, no Lifecycle), :1296-1306 (terminationGrace)
- FACT: `lenny-adapter prestop` SIGTERMs the adapter first and then waits; the SIGTERM goroutine already calls lifecycle.Close() before srv.GracefulStop(), so CH-RUNTIMEOPS is closed at the start of the preStop window today. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-adapter/prestop.go:67-92, cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:413-427
- WATCHOUT: the adapter cannot SIGTERM/SIGKILL the sidecar runtime (no shared PID namespace), yet staged SPEC-12(b) and SPEC-13 give it that escalation at pod termination. The false text is pre-existing in §28 (terminate row, heartbeat, shutdown) and was copied into new staged text. EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:616, :624, :1082
- WATCHOUT: §15.4.3 still has a per-session CH-MSGSOCK `shutdown` for Basic runtimes (at session expiry and as the Basic interrupt), which a kept process must not receive. The code sends no `shutdown` frame (grep of pkg/adapter), so this is spec-only. EVIDENCE: spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1720, :1783, :1795, :1800
- FACT: a running session that ends in failure retires its pod regardless of recycle settings, so the hung-runtime row (heartbeat -> Interrupt no-op -> failed session -> retire) holds on sequential pools. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:471 ("A session that ends in failure or a crash always retires its pod")
- FACT: the failed-start drain citations in D4 resolve: bindlaunch.go StartSession failure -> reclaim -> reclaimOnFailure -> failPhase -> drain (reclaim.go:28-60); concurrent path accountSlotFailure drains at the §5.2 threshold (slot_bind.go:183-190). Reserved rebind goes through Claimer.Claim (claimer.go:118, rebindReserved) and so through the same launch path.
- FACT: the fallback claim runs only while the apiserver is reachable (reachability probe before ClaimIdle), so AdmitTenantPin's Pod read and the new pin stamp are not blocked by the fallback's own precondition. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/fallbackclaim.go:81-87

### [spec.2.fix-design-G3.1]
- DECISION: §29.2's CH-MSGSOCK accept is traced at step 25 (tuple `runtime` → `adapter`, `CH-MSGSOCK`, `intra-pod`), and step 29 becomes an `adapter`, `internal` step that points back to step 25 and states no timing — BECAUSE §29.1 numbers steps in the order they occur (spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:55-56) and the accept happens at §4.7.9 step 7, before 26a-28 — ALTERNATIVES: keep step 29 as the message step and drop "at step 25" (rejected: position 29 still asserts the accept follows the CH-RUNTIMEOPS handshake); delete step 29 and renumber 30+ (rejected: renumbers steps other sections cite).
- DECISION: SPEC-12(b) step 13 gets the tuple `adapter`, `internal` — BECAUSE the step records the absence of any frame or signal, which is an event inside the adapter, and §29.1 (spec/29:77-78) gives that form to a step inside one participant.
- WATCHOUT: every §29 step opens with a participant tuple (`internal`, an arrow tuple, `unstated`, `no register entry`, or a REG- id). A rewrite that deletes a message tuple must put the `internal` form in its place.


### [spec.2.follow-up-fix.1]
CORRECTS [spec.2.fix-G3]: the new SPEC-12(a) step 25 kept the `runtime` → `adapter`, `CH-MSGSOCK` tuple under a condition (any pod-warm `type: agent` runtime) that covers later sessions and embedded pods, where no dial occurs. Step 25 now opens "On the first session of a pod-warm pod whose `type: agent` runtime runs in the sidecar deployment model" and carries no later-session clause. Step 29 (`adapter`, `internal`) states both models: sidecar serves the session on the pod's connection dialled at step 25 on the first session; embedded runs the loop in the adapter's process (§4.7.9 step 7).
DECISION: scoped step 29 per deployment model rather than taking the suggested generic step 29 text — BECAUSE the suggested text still asserted a `CH-MSGSOCK` connection on embedded pods, the same defect the finding named — ALTERNATIVES: a separate embedded-only step (adds a number; one `internal` step with two clauses each stating its condition satisfies §29.1 **Conditional steps.**).
WATCHOUT: §29.1 **Step numbering.** and **Conditional steps.** (spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:57-65) require a message tuple only where the message occurs under the step's full condition; a trace step that covers first and later sessions of a recycled pod cannot carry the first-session dial. EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:57-71
FACT: spec-changes.md "Out of scope" manifest-ordering bullet described step 25 as stating "the runtime's lifetime"; rewritten to "the runtime's `CH-MSGSOCK` dial" because the lifetime now lives in step 29 and §4.7.10.
DEFERRED [implementation-checklist.md]: step S3 names "§29.2 step 25" but SPEC-12(a) also replaces §29.2 step 29 (since r1); the S3 site list should read "§29.2 steps 25 and 29".

### [spec.2.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no spec-scoped findings under the performance and failure-mode lens this round — BECAUSE the new SPEC-8(c) pinned-idle accounting (toward maxWarm, not minWarm, excess drain) bounds idle inventory at maxWarm, which §17 pods_floor sums, and its per-session pod churn on multi-tenant pools is no worse than a non-recycling pool that the tier sizing already budgets; the maxWarm == minWarm consequence is an already-fixed item — ALTERNATIVES: filing the PSC feedback loop (lower reuse lowers mode_factor, which raises minWarm and shrinks the pinned cap) was rejected because it is bounded at maxWarm and degrades to non-recycling behavior rather than past it.
FACT: the WarmPoolController publishes lenny_warmpool_idle_pods and accrues lenny_warmpool_idle_pod_minutes from Plan.ReadyCount — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/controller.go:305, :313. CODE-10 removes pinned idle pods from ReadyCount, so both series drop pinned idle pods. For WarmPoolExhausted and WarmPoolLow (pkg/alerting/rules/rules.go:283, :687) that is the right reading of unpinned inventory. For the cost counter (spec/16_observability.md:139, spec/04_system-components.md:434) it under-reports the idle pods a pool is paying for.
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md CODE-10]: "is not counted in WarmCount or ReadyCount" also removes pinned idle pods from lenny_warmpool_idle_pod_minutes, because controller.go:305 feeds r.idle.observe from decision.ReadyCount. What is true instead: the idle-cost counter should accrue pinned idle pods too, for example by feeding it ReadyCount + PinnedIdleCount. The non-spec loop should decide this.
OPEN: the non-spec fallback pin check (non-spec-changes.md:25, :78) runs AdmitTenantPin, an apiserver Pod read, inside ClaimIdle's SELECT ... FOR UPDATE transaction, once for each refused row. The fallback activates only when the apiserver watch stream is degraded (spec/04_system-components.md:440-444), so the row locks and the Postgres connection are held for the full latency of a degraded apiserver. The non-spec performance lens should decide whether to filter on the mirror row's tenant_id in SQL first.
WATCHOUT: StampDrainRequest writes an RFC3339Nano timestamp value (pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go:95-107), so each re-stamp is a real etcd write and wakes the pod watch. Several other-tenant claims can scan past the same pinned pod before the controller drains it. Whether to skip the stamp when the annotation is already present is a code-loop question.


### [spec.2.review-reliability.1]

- FACT: a heartbeat-hung runtime on a `maxConcurrentSessions: 1` pool still retires the pod, because §5.2 retires the pod on any session that ends in failure ("A session that ends in failure or a crash always retires its pod regardless of recycle settings"). On a concurrent pool, the hung runtime is bounded by the Slot retry policy's whole-pod replacement trigger and by `maxSessionsPerPod`. SPEC-14 removing SIGTERM therefore leaves no unbounded reuse. — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:471, :508, :577
- FACT: a runtime that stops during the `reserved` hold fails the next `StartSession`. That start failure is a pre-attached failure, which the gateway retries on a new pod, so the client sees no failure. — EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:285; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/bindlaunch.go reclaim on StartSession error
- FACT: the adapter's heartbeat monitor is per Attach stream (per session), so "that session's stream" in SPEC-14(a) matches the code. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/attach.go:75, pkg/adapter/heartbeat.go:163
- FACT: `plan.Compute`'s warm target is `minWarm` clamped to `maxWarm`, so CODE-10's "MaxWarm minus the target" matches SPEC-8(c)'s "maxWarm minus minWarm". — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/plan/plan.go warmTarget
- WATCHOUT: the §6.2 `idle ──→ draining` edge lists neither the existing scale-down drain nor SPEC-8(c)'s pinned-excess drain. The scale-down omission predates this proposal, so the missing pinned drain is not a new inconsistency. — EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:98-100; spec/04_system-components.md:424-426
- UNVERIFIED: the pinned-excess drain on a pool whose `maxWarm` equals `minWarm` fires as the hold expires, which is the moment the pinned tenant's next claim is most likely. Whether `patchSandboxDraining(requireIdle)` fences a claim created before its projection is a code or Kubernetes-lens question, not a spec edit. — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/pod_reconciler.go patchSandboxDraining comment

### [spec.4.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-1's later-session sentence now skips §4.7.9 steps 7, 8, and 9, and SPEC-12(a) rescopes §29.2 steps 26a, 26b, 26c, 27, and 28 to `On the first session of a pod-warm pod`, per-step — BECAUSE a kept runtime opens MCP connections and CH-RUNTIMEOPS and runs the lifecycle_capabilities handshake once per pod (pkg/adapter/runtimeops.go `Run` handshakes once per accepted connection), and §29.1 **Conditional steps.** requires each step to carry its own condition — ALTERNATIVES: one scoping sentence above 26a (violates §29.1); adding a sidecar-model qualifier to 26a-28 (embedded opens neither channel, so it changes nothing built); renumbering 25-28 into one block (breaks citers of step 25).
DECISION: §15.4.1's "the runtime knows it's receiving its first message by virtue of just having started" is deleted (SPEC-11(d)) with no replacement predicate; `No `sessionState` field.` stays — BECAUSE the only home for how a kept runtime tells sessions apart is SPEC-5(b) ("keyed by the frame's `sessionId`").
WATCHOUT: do not stage "a `message` whose `sessionId` the runtime has not yet seen is that session's first message" in §15.4.1. A slot retry lands "to a new slot on the same pod" (§5.2 **Slot retry policy**), and a resume can reach the same kept process, so an already-seen sessionId opens a new slot. The predicate is false. — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md (Slot retry policy paragraph)
WATCHOUT: SPEC-11's new §15.4.1 part is lettered (d), appended after (c), so SPEC-11(b) citers (open decision 5 in spec-changes, the DRAINING row) stay valid. Do not re-letter.
FACT: all five §29.2 steps 26a, 26b, 26c, 27, and 28 open with the literal `On a pod-warm pod with ...`, so the SPEC-12(a) substitution is mechanical. — EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md steps 26a-28 under §29.2

DECISION: §4.7.9 per-pod scope goes in SPEC-1's one sentence ("skips this step and steps 8 and 9, and uses the connections the runtime opened on the pod's first session"), and §29.2 steps 26a, 26b, 26c, 27, 28 each change their opening `On a pod-warm pod` to `On the first session of a pod-warm pod` via one instruction sentence appended to SPEC-12(a) — BECAUSE §29.1 **Conditional steps.** requires each step to carry its full condition, and the steps cite §4.7, whose §4.7.9 then states the skip — ALTERNATIVES: a single scoping sentence above 26a (violates §29.1 per-step rule); deleting SPEC-1's sentence (leaves steps 8-9 unconditioned against SPEC-2).
DECISION: §15.4.1 takes a pure deletion of the clause ` — the runtime knows it's receiving its first message by virtue of just having started`, staged as SPEC-11(d), leaving `No `sessionState` field.` — BECAUSE the reason is false once the process is kept (and already false on concurrent pods), and the proposal's one home for how later sessions are told apart is SPEC-5(b) ("keyed by the frame's `sessionId`") — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's predicate "a `message` whose `sessionId` the runtime has not yet seen is that session's first message" is a false universal: §5.2 **Slot retry policy** assigns a retry "to a new slot on the same pod" (spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:571), and a resumed session can be placed on a recycled idle pod pinned to its tenant, so a seen `sessionId` can open a new slot on the same kept process.

WATCHOUT: append §15.4.1 as SPEC-11(d), never as a new (b): open decision 5 and the D8/review findings cite SPEC-11(b) for the DRAINING row. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:498.

- DECISION: step 1b's reach is stated in step 1b itself (replace "Purge all `shmget`-allocated IPC shared memory segments" with "Purge the `shmget`-allocated IPC shared memory segments the adapter's UID owns or created"); SPEC-6(b) drops its step-1b sentence and SPEC-6(c) drops its second replacement, so the pre-existing parenthetical "(... are addressed by step 1b above.)" stays as a bare pointer — BECAUSE the step owns its own predicate, and rounds 1-3 kept the rule in "What the scrub reaches" while the step's universal stood beside it — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's appended pointer "It removes only the segments 'What the scrub reaches' names" leaves "Purge all" and "only" in one step (a qualifier, the same answer one step smaller); keeping a "Step 1b's reach is stated in step 1b" pointer inside SPEC-6(b) is hair.
- FACT: the adapter UID and the agent UID are distinct (spec/13 §13.1 "Cross-UID file delivery", `pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go` constants), and pods share one IPC namespace, so a non-root `ipcrm --all=shm` fails with EPERM on the runtime UID's segments in the sidecar model. - WATCHOUT: `pkg/adapter/scrub/defaultops.go` `PurgeIPCShm` doc comment says "removes every System V shared-memory segment". - FACT: nothing in spec/, docs/, or tests/tier11_docs pins the phrase "Purge all `shmget`"; the step-1b replacement breaks no gate. D11, summary:29, and problem-statement:31 describe the adapter-UID reach and stay true.

### [spec.4.review-citations.1]

- DECISION: filed one citation finding. New §29.9 step 4b (SPEC-12(f)) states the pod-exit `terminate` frame carries the reason `eviction` and cites the §4.7 `Shutdown` row and §28.5.3 `CH-RUNTIMEOPS`. Neither states a reason: SPEC-3(a) says only "the adapter sends it once, when the pod terminates", and the §28 terminate row lists `eviction` as one enum value. The value lives only in D8, which does not land in spec/. TEST-3 and TEST-17 assert `eviction` under `spec_15_4_2`. BECAUSE §29.1 forbids a trace from supplying behaviour that no section states. ### [spec.4.review-kubernetes.1]

- FACT: the new recycle-boundary retires need no new projection input. `ProjectOccupancyPhase` never reads the pool or the scrub report; it maps the gateway-written claim disposition `released`/`failed` to `draining`, so SPEC-4(b)/SPEC-9(b) "after its scrub report" ride the existing vm-restart path unchanged — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/occupancy.go:84-140

- FACT: `reconcileDrainRequest` calls `patchSandboxDraining(requireIdle=false)`, so it drains an idle pod carrying the tenant-change stamp. The OccupancyReconciler maps a `bound` claim to `claimed` with no sticky `draining`, so a drain-requested pod that is bound flips back to claimed. This affects the existing unhealthy-threshold drain as well, and the staged spec does not cause it — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/pod_reconciler.go:609-621, pkg/controller/warmpool/occupancy.go:86-88,204-207
- WATCHOUT: the webhook's default branch refuses `unassigned → {tenant_id}`, so a pod reset to `unassigned` can never be re-pinned, and AdmitTenantPin refuses it. - USEFUL [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]: the FACT that the runtime container has no preStop hook and gets the kubelet SIGTERM at T0 settled the D8 ordering question. Open decision 5 owns it, and no staged spec text asserts an order.

### [spec.4.review-mechanism.1]

- FACT: the Full-level CH-RUNTIMEOPS handshake runs once per runtime connection rather than once per session. `RuntimeOps.Run` serves one connection, handshakes, and re-handshakes only after that runtime disconnects. Under D8 and D10 the connection closes only at pod exit, so a kept runtime never re-opens CH-RUNTIMEOPS for a later session. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/runtimeops.go:161-198, spec-changes.md:21 (D8)

SPEC-1 scopes the later-session skip to §4.7.9 step 7 alone, and SPEC-12(a) scopes §29.2 step 25 to the first session. §4.7.9 steps 8-9 (open CH-RUNTIMEOPS, lifecycle_capabilities/lifecycle_support) and §29.2 steps 26c, 27, and 28 still describe per-session events that happen once per kept process — BECAUSE this is newly false on sequential recycling pools, and the new staged text states it by implication — ALTERNATIVES: judging it pre-existing, since §29.10 already claims §29.2 holds per slot on a concurrent pod. I did not choose that, because SPEC-1's new sentence adds an explicit statement.
- WATCHOUT: the pod MCP surface (26a/26b) is cancelled at the cohort close and re-armed at the next start (`takePodMCPCancelsLocked`, pkg/adapter/slotsession.go:150-165), so whether a kept runtime reconnects to MCP on a later session has no single answer. The finding is limited to CH-RUNTIMEOPS. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slotsession.go:163

- USEFUL [spec.3.review-mechanism.1]: its WATCHOUT on step 29 vs 26a-28 pointed at the ordering. The ordering is below the bar. The per-session CH-RUNTIMEOPS claim is not.

### [spec.4.review-performance.1]

FACT: `lenny_warmpool_idle_pods` is published from the warm-pool planner's `ReadyCount` (`pkg/controller/warmpool/controller.go:313`, `setIdlePods(pool.Name, decision.ReadyCount)`), and the idle-cost counter observes the same value (`controller.go:305`). CODE-10 removes pinned idle pods from `ReadyCount`, so it silently changes both series. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-10 ("is not counted in `WarmCount` or `ReadyCount`").
FACT: the gateway's claim path uses an uncached client (`cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go:169`, `client.New`), so `Claimer.Claim` already issues a direct Sandbox LIST per claim, and `AdmitTenantPin` adds one direct Pod GET per scanned candidate. No new informer or watch is created on the gateway. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go:104-108.
FACT: the recycle counters (`sessions_served`, `scrub_failure_count`) live in Postgres `agent_pod_state`. A Postgres error during `RecordPodScrub` propagates, so the pod retires through the report timeout (fail closed). The new `Decide` inputs add no Redis dependency. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/leasecontrol/scrubreport_server.go:504-512, :558-564.
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: CODE-10 drops pinned idle pods from `ReadyCount`, and that value also feeds `r.idle.observe` (`lenny_warmpool_idle_pod_minutes`, the idle-cost counter behind `WarmPoolIdleCostHigh`). As staged, pinned idle pods, up to maxWarm minus minWarm per pool, stop counting toward idle cost. Keep `ReadyCount + PinnedIdleCount` in the idle-cost observation. What the gauge should report depends on how the spec finding on the §16.1 `lenny_warmpool_idle_pods` row is resolved.
OPEN: on an `allowCrossTenantReuse` pool, SPEC-8(c) drains every pinned pod that a claim's scan refuses, not only the first. When no admissible pod exists, one claim from another tenant drains the pool's entire pinned idle inventory. It is not reported, because the replacements add unpinned capacity during exhaustion and the result is consistent with D6's "would reach" wording. A human may want "at most one drain per acquisition".

### [spec.4.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no reliability finding against the staged spec edits in round 4 — BECAUSE every recovery path the staging relies on already exists and closes within a bound: a sequential failed session retires its pod (§5.2 Recycle lifecycle, "A session that ends in failure or a crash always retires its pod"), a concurrent failed slot start counts toward the whole-pod trigger (sessionserver accountSlotFailure), and a runtime that dies in reserved or idle is found by the next claim's fail-fast Start — ALTERNATIVES: a boundary heartbeat probe, or proactive adapter reporting of `ended`. Both would only recover faster, which is below the bar.
FACT: the reserved-hold dispatch goes through Claimer.rebindReserved inside Claim, so a failed start there reaches Binder.failPhase and drains, the same as the idle path — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go:118,189; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/reclaim.go (failPhase)
FACT: agent pods render no readiness or liveness probe, and the WarmPoolController reads no container status, so an idle pod whose runtime container exited is found only at the next claim — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go (no Probe fields); pkg/controller/warmpool
FACT: the heartbeat monitor runs per Attach session rather than per connection, so SPEC-14(a)'s "ends that session's stream" matches the code — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/attach.go:75,146-151
FACT: the plan Drain goes through patchSandboxDraining with requireIdle=false. If the pinned-idle drain (CODE-10) races a claim, it can drain a pod that a session has just claimed. This is code-level and out of scope for the spec loop — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/controller.go:280-283,641
UNVERIFIED: whether the slothealth.Tracker for the whole-pod replacement trigger is per gateway replica (in-memory). If it is, SPEC-7(a)'s concurrent-pool "pod drains when that trigger fires" is slower across replicas, but it stays bounded. The non-spec loop or the performance lens should check this.

### [spec.4.review-security.1]
DECISION: no security findings on the r3->r4 diff or the rest of the staging — BECAUSE the diff (SPEC-4(g) pointer, SPEC-11(b) diagram, SPEC-12(a)/(b) step tuples, §9.1 wording) touches no control, and every bound the staging introduces has a trusted source: the acknowledgment is read from the Postgres pool record at the boundary (fail-closed on missing pool label, error propagates; pkg/gateway/session/recycle/scrubreporter_seams.go:600-615), sessions_served is a Postgres agent_pod_state column (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:494), the pin is a webhook-guarded Pod label, and runtime_live from the adapter can only retire or allow the reuse the pool already acknowledged — ALTERNATIVES: re-filing the SPEC-13 full-revoke / session-expiry "runtime keeps running" outcome (earlier security rounds rejected it as merely less strict, since concurrent pools already keep the shared runtime); filing the missing ConcurrentWorkspaceCredentialSharing-style pool condition for acknowledged sequential pools (not a removed control; the client disclosure covers it).
FACT: the claim paths fail closed when the tenant stamp fails. Claimer.Claim returns an error after stampPodTenant fails (it tolerates only NotFound), so a TOCTOU between the pin read and the CREATE cannot dispatch a session across tenants: the webhook refuses the B->A relabel and the claim errors. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go:150-157, pkg/admission/label_immutability/label_immutability.go:197-205
USEFUL [spec.3 security DECISION on SPEC-4(d) unassigned reset]: confirmed again that the webhook forbids unassigned->{tenant}, so the ungated WPC reset can make a pod unclaimable but cannot move it across tenants. Do not re-file.

### [f1.other-proposals]
DECISION: the 0084 impact item is recorded as a row under a new `## Impacts on other proposals` section at the end of the summary. The row states that CODE-3's deletion of the generation reset leaves `soleSession` empty for every session after a kept process's first, including on `maxConcurrentSessions: 1` pools, so 0084's unaddressed "since it last served none" arm refuses unaddressed MCP calls and drops unaddressed token folds on a recycled pod's later sessions, while its addressed `runtimeSession` arm and the `callingSession` deletion survive. 0084 is Draft, drafted 2026-09-26. FACT: no test pins "serves more than one session" or "reuses pods across executions" (grep over tests/, pkg/, and schemas/ returned no hits), so the rewording is safe from tier-11.

### [spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: §4.7.9 per-pod scope goes in SPEC-1's one sentence ("skips this step and steps 8 and 9, and uses the connections the runtime opened on the pod's first session"), and §29.2 steps 26a, 26b, 26c, 27, 28 each change their opening `On a pod-warm pod` to `On the first session of a pod-warm pod` via one instruction sentence appended to SPEC-12(a) — BECAUSE §29.1 **Conditional steps.** requires each step to carry its full condition, and the steps cite §4.7, whose §4.7.9 then states the skip — ALTERNATIVES: a single scoping sentence above 26a (violates §29.1 per-step rule); deleting SPEC-1's sentence (leaves steps 8-9 unconditioned against SPEC-2).
DECISION: §15.4.1 takes a pure deletion of the clause ` — the runtime knows it's receiving its first message by virtue of just having started`, staged as SPEC-11(d), leaving `No `sessionState` field.` — BECAUSE the reason is false once the process is kept (and already false on concurrent pods), and the proposal's one home for how later sessions are told apart is SPEC-5(b) ("keyed by the frame's `sessionId`") — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's predicate "a `message` whose `sessionId` the runtime has not yet seen is that session's first message" is a false universal: §5.2 **Slot retry policy** assigns a retry "to a new slot on the same pod" (spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:571), and a resumed session can be placed on a recycled idle pod pinned to its tenant, so a seen `sessionId` can open a new slot on the same kept process.
WATCHOUT: steps 24 and 29 of §29.2 also open `On a pod-warm pod` and are per-session; a replace-all of that prefix wrongly scopes them. EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:292 (step 24), SPEC-12(a) new step 29.
WATCHOUT: append §15.4.1 as SPEC-11(d), never as a new (b): open decision 5 and the D8/review findings cite SPEC-11(b) for the DRAINING row. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:498.
FACT: the embedded path opens neither CH-RUNTIMEOPS nor intra-pod MCP; `NewRuntimeOps` is wired only in cmd/lenny-adapter. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:371-373; no hits in pkg/adapter/embedded.go.
FACT: `RuntimeOps.Run` handshakes once per accepted runtime connection and keeps the listener for the pod, so a kept runtime handshakes once per pod. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/runtimeops.go:161-198.
