The follow-up manifest-ordering proposal should scope it. EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:555-560.

### [spec.4.fix-design-G1.2]
DECISION: the §4.7 `Shutdown` row (SPEC-3(a)) is the one home of the pod-exit `terminate` send condition (once, reason `eviction`, only to a runtime that opened `CH-RUNTIMEOPS`, never at a session's teardown); SPEC-11(b)'s `DRAINING` row names the frame and points to that row, and SPEC-12(f) drops its recipient sentence and "once" — BECAUSE the current spec already homes the signal's condition in that row and SPEC-12(b), SPEC-12(f), and SPEC-13 already point there — ALTERNATIVES: homing it in §15.4.2 `DRAINING` (the state is pod-level, but moving the home re-targets three pointers and D8), rejected; re-synchronising the copies, rejected by the single-home rule.
DECISION: §10.1.4 **Hold state timeout:** gets its own SPEC-18, a pure deletion of `it sends `terminate` on the CH-RUNTIMEOPS and ` — BECAUSE the hold timeout tears down sessions on a pod whose adapter keeps running, so after SPEC-3(a) the clause is a second, contradictory send point; the code already sends nothing there — ALTERNATIVES: replacing the clause with a pointer to the §4.7 `Shutdown` row, rejected (the deletion leaves nothing a reader could misread).
FACT: the hold-timeout loop sends no CH-RUNTIMEOPS frame and does not exit the adapter process. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/holdstate.go `terminateHeldSession` doc comment ("The loop sends no CH-RUNTIMEOPS drain signal"), `onHoldTimeout` (no os.Exit, no SIGTERM).
WATCHOUT: §10.1.4 also says "Before exiting, the adapter SHOULD send a final `AdapterTerminating`" and that the orphan reconciler "detects the terminated pod", which implies the adapter exits at hold timeout; the code does not. If a later round reads that as a pod termination, the pod-exit frame (D8: SIGTERM handler only) would not fire on a self-exit. That divergence predates 0079 and is not this group's to stage. EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md §10.1.4 **Hold state timeout:**; pkg/adapter/holdstate.go `onHoldTimeout`.
OPEN: no test asserts that the hold timeout writes no `terminate` frame; `TestCoordinatorHoldTimeoutRecoversTheNextSession_spec_10_1` asserts only the next session's teardown. Whether TEST-3 gains a one-line hold-timeout no-frame assertion is for a non-spec round.
DEFERRED [0079_fix_name-who-starts-the-next-sessions-runtime-on-a-recycled-pod.implementation-checklist.md]: step S3 lists "SPEC-9, SPEC-10, SPEC-11, SPEC-12, SPEC-13, SPEC-14, SPEC-15, SPEC-16, and SPEC-17"; once SPEC-18 (spec/10 §10.1.4) is staged, S3 must list SPEC-18 as well, written out rather than as a range, or implement-proposal-build narrows the spec/ lease and omits spec/10.


### [spec.4.fix-design-G2.1]

- DECISION: step 1b's reach is stated in step 1b itself (replace "Purge all `shmget`-allocated IPC shared memory segments" with "Purge the `shmget`-allocated IPC shared memory segments the adapter's UID owns or created"); SPEC-6(b) drops its step-1b sentence and SPEC-6(c) drops its second replacement, so the pre-existing parenthetical "(... are addressed by step 1b above.)" stays as a bare pointer — BECAUSE the step owns its own predicate, and rounds 1-3 kept the rule in "What the scrub reaches" while the step's universal stood beside it — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's appended pointer "It removes only the segments 'What the scrub reaches' names" leaves "Purge all" and "only" in one step (a qualifier, the same answer one step smaller); keeping a "Step 1b's reach is stated in step 1b" pointer inside SPEC-6(b) is hair.
- FACT: the adapter UID and the agent UID are distinct (spec/13 §13.1 "Cross-UID file delivery", `pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go` constants), and pods share one IPC namespace, so a non-root `ipcrm --all=shm` fails with EPERM on the runtime UID's segments in the sidecar model. In the embedded model the runtime is the adapter's UID, so its segments are marked for removal and freed only on last detach; the kept process is unaffected. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/scrub/defaultops.go PurgeIPCShm; spec/13_security-model.md:26
- WATCHOUT: `pkg/adapter/scrub/defaultops.go` `PurgeIPCShm` doc comment says "removes every System V shared-memory segment". That is a non-spec-loop finding (restage the comment in non-spec-changes against the amended step 1b), not part of this spec fix. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/scrub/defaultops.go:37
- FACT: nothing in spec/, docs/, or tests/tier11_docs pins the phrase "Purge all `shmget`"; the step-1b replacement breaks no gate. D11, summary:29, and problem-statement:31 describe the adapter-UID reach and stay true.

### [spec.4.fix-design-G2.2]

- DECISION: the podReuse/residualStateWarning definitions become pool-configuration statements at their two spec homes: SPEC-8(e) gains a second replacement in the §5.2 **Client visibility of weak-isolation reuse.** sentence (`the session runs on a pod that serves more than one session over its lifetime` -> `the session runs on a pool configured to reuse pods`), and SPEC-10 gains a deletion in the §7.1 `podReuse` row (`when the assigned pool reuses pods across executions: `), leaving the configuration list that the `residualStateWarning` row already uses — BECAUSE after SPEC-7(a) an unacknowledged recycling pool retires each pod after one session while isolationlevel.go still reports both fields `true` from `match.Recycle`, so both behaviour-worded definitions are false on the default recycling configuration — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's replace in the podReuse row ("is configured to reuse pods across executions:") is longer than a deletion that leaves the same configuration list; deleting the §5.2 lead clause outright leaves "When `residualStateWarning: true`, on a recycling pod ..." ungrammatical; a new field or an acknowledgment input to `scrubPolicyForPool` re-opens D12.
- DECISION: D12 and the `sessionIsolationLevel` edge row stay unchanged — BECAUSE "over-warns" remains true in substance (no residual state from a prior session on a pod that retires after one session) and the edge row already names SPEC-8 and SPEC-10 as the landing sites.

None is a site. No tests/tier11_docs file pins "serves more than one session" or "reuses pods across". — EVIDENCE: docs/api/rest.md:78, docs/api/mcp.md:160
- WATCHOUT: docs/client-guide/session-lifecycle.md:404 ("the pod is reused across sequential sessions of the same tenant") is a behaviour claim that SPEC-7(a) falsifies for an unacknowledged pool; it is outside this fix and no DOC deliverable names it. - DECISION: no findings on the post-redesign staging (redesign1.apply.2) under the fresh holistic lens — BECAUSE every relative pointer the staged §5.2 text uses ("above"/"below") resolves to the right side of its target (tenant pinning :442, onScrubFailure :497, Pod retirement policy :502, validation paragraph :527, concurrent acknowledgment :533, mode_factor :592), every SPEC-4/SPEC-9/SPEC-12 anchor resolves once, and the SPEC-8(c) counts match plan.Compute (unpinned converge to warmTarget, pinned bounded at clamped maxWarm − target, so total idle ≤ maxWarm) — ALTERNATIVES: (1) SPEC-1's "A later session on the same pod skips this step and steps 8 and 9" vs its own closing "In the embedded deployment model ... for each session": rejected, the skip sentence sits inside the sidecar narration and reads as sidecar-scoped; (2) SPEC-8(c)'s "It leaves inventory when ..." list omitting the allowCrossTenantReuse tenant-change drain stated earlier in the same block: rejected as an enumeration completeness gap of the kind r4 refuted for §29.2 step 32.

- WATCHOUT: spec/04 §4.6.1 CRD validation says `maxWarm > 0` (spec/04_system-components.md:436) while §10.7 concluded sets `maxWarm` to 0 (spec/10_gateway-internals.md:1121). D14's "at minWarm = maxWarm = 0" relies on the latter. This contradiction predates 0079; do not file it against D14.

### [spec.4.review-kubernetes.1]

- FACT: the new recycle-boundary retires need no new projection input. `ProjectOccupancyPhase` never reads the pool or the scrub report; it maps the gateway-written claim disposition `released`/`failed` to `draining`, so SPEC-4(b)/SPEC-9(b) "after its scrub report" ride the existing vm-restart path unchanged — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/occupancy.go:84-140
- FACT: "the gateway provisions a fresh replacement" (SPEC-7(a) new items) is not an RBAC violation. It copies §5.2 step 7's wording, "provisions a fresh replacement pod from the warm pool", which means the gateway claims a warm pod rather than creating one — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:493, spec/04_system-components.md:632
- FACT: `reconcileDrainRequest` calls `patchSandboxDraining(requireIdle=false)`, so it drains an idle pod carrying the tenant-change stamp. The OccupancyReconciler maps a `bound` claim to `claimed` with no sticky `draining`, so a drain-requested pod that is bound flips back to claimed. This affects the existing unhealthy-threshold drain as well, and the staged spec does not cause it — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/pod_reconciler.go:609-621, pkg/controller/warmpool/occupancy.go:86-88,204-207
- WATCHOUT: the webhook's default branch refuses `unassigned → {tenant_id}`, so a pod reset to `unassigned` can never be re-pinned, and AdmitTenantPin refuses it. The WPC reset that SPEC-4(d) leaves admitted cannot enable cross-tenant reuse. It can only strand the pod — EVIDENCE: pkg/admission/label_immutability/label_immutability.go:196-205
- DECISION: I did not file the §28.5.3/§15.4.3 "adapter sends SIGTERM/SIGKILL on terminate/shutdown deadline" sites against SPEC-1's "share no process namespace". BECAUSE §13.1's shareProcessNamespace ban and the separate sidecar runtime container already contradicted them before this proposal, so SPEC-1 only makes the conflict explicit. ALTERNATIVES: filing it as (d). Rejected as pre-existing, and the review log (:133, :381, :397) records it as a separate finding.
- USEFUL [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]: the FACT that the runtime container has no preStop hook and gets the kubelet SIGTERM at T0 settled the D8 ordering question. Open decision 5 owns it, and no staged spec text asserts an order.

### [spec.4.review-kubernetes.2]

- DECISION: no Kubernetes-idiom findings against the staged spec edits in the re-run of round 4 — BECAUSE every new write stays with its owner: pinned-idle accounting and drains are WarmPoolController writes to `SandboxWarmPool.status` and `Sandbox.status`, computed level-triggered from Pod labels, SandboxClaim existence, and PSC-owned `spec.minWarm`/`maxWarm`; the gateway writes only its own SandboxClaim and the Pod tenant label and drain-request annotation its RBAC already grants (spec/04_system-components.md:632); `runtime_live` travels on the adapter RPC rather than through any status field — ALTERNATIVES: filing SPEC-7(a)'s "the gateway drains the pod" as an ownership violation (rejected: the gateway's drain is a claim DELETE and the WPC projects `draining`, pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/reclaim.go:87-91); filing the `lenny.dev/state: idle` PDB (spec/04:511) counting pinned idle pods toward `minWarm - 1` (rejected: §4.6.1 already accepts surplus idle pods admitting more evictions, and proactive replacement restores unpinned `minWarm`).
- FACT: `idlePodCount` in the admin API and the `PoolWarmingUp` 503 gate are both read from `SandboxWarmPool.status.readyCount` and the SandboxTemplate condition, so SPEC-8(c)'s "as do the `idlePodCount` and the `PoolWarmingUp` condition derived from them" matches the code — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resolve.go:434, :448; pkg/controller/warmpool/controller.go:313
- FACT: the planner's excess drain converges unpinned idle pods to the target (not to `maxWarm`), so with pinned idle pods bounded at `maxWarm - target`, total idle stays at or below `maxWarm`, which is what SPEC-8(c)'s "counts toward maxWarm" claims — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/plan/plan.go:128-141
- WATCHOUT: the OccupancyReconciler picks the no-claim edge from the Sandbox's current phase (`reserved` goes to idle, `claimed` goes to draining). A failed-start claim DELETE after a `reserved → bound` rebind that lands before the projection writes `claimed` would return a pod with a dead runtime to `idle` instead of draining it. D4's fail-fast `Start` narrows the window between the rebind patch and the DELETE. The mechanism predates this proposal, and a fix would be in code (write the `failed` disposition rather than DELETE, or make the edge independent of phase), so it was not filed in the spec loop — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/occupancy.go:124-141; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/reclaim.go:51-61, :87-91; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go:118-121
- UNVERIFIED: whether any production path calls StartSession immediately after `rebindReserved` (a one-shot create-and-start) so that the WATCHOUT above is reachable. The non-spec loop's reliability or mechanism lens should check this.
- USEFUL [spec.1.review-kubernetes.1]: its FACTs (uncached gateway client; unconditional WPC reset in the webhook) and its WATCHOUT on the two WPC phase writers settled three candidate findings without re-deriving them.
- USEFUL [spec.2.review-kubernetes.1]: the non-exhaustive `idle ──→ draining` edge and the idle-drain vs claim-CREATE race are pre-existing; do not re-file them against SPEC-8(c) or SPEC-9(e).


### [spec.4.review-mechanism.1]

- FACT: the Full-level CH-RUNTIMEOPS handshake runs once per runtime connection rather than once per session. `RuntimeOps.Run` serves one connection, handshakes, and re-handshakes only after that runtime disconnects. Under D8 and D10 the connection closes only at pod exit, so a kept runtime never re-opens CH-RUNTIMEOPS for a later session. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/runtimeops.go:161-198, spec-changes.md:21 (D8)
- DECISION: filed one finding. SPEC-1 scopes the later-session skip to §4.7.9 step 7 alone, and SPEC-12(a) scopes §29.2 step 25 to the first session. §4.7.9 steps 8-9 (open CH-RUNTIMEOPS, lifecycle_capabilities/lifecycle_support) and §29.2 steps 26c, 27, and 28 still describe per-session events that happen once per kept process — BECAUSE this is newly false on sequential recycling pools, and the new staged text states it by implication — ALTERNATIVES: judging it pre-existing, since §29.10 already claims §29.2 holds per slot on a concurrent pod. I did not choose that, because SPEC-1's new sentence adds an explicit statement.
- WATCHOUT: the pod MCP surface (26a/26b) is cancelled at the cohort close and re-armed at the next start (`takePodMCPCancelsLocked`, pkg/adapter/slotsession.go:150-165), so whether a kept runtime reconnects to MCP on a later session has no single answer. The finding is limited to CH-RUNTIMEOPS. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slotsession.go:163
- FACT: RecordPodScrub calls InspectForRecycle at report time, so SPEC-7(a)'s "reads the field when the whole-pod scrub reports" matches the code. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/leasecontrol/scrubreport_server.go:504
- USEFUL [spec.3.review-mechanism.1]: its WATCHOUT on step 29 vs 26a-28 pointed at the ordering. The ordering is below the bar. The per-session CH-RUNTIMEOPS claim is not.

### [spec.4.review-mechanism.2]

- DECISION: no mechanism finding against the staged spec edits after redesign1.apply.2 — BECAUSE each flow traced closes: the boundary report (SPEC-3(c), SPEC-7(a)) is sampled after the scrub and fails closed on an omitted field; the failed-start drain covers a runtime that stops during the hold on both pool concurrencies; the pin read (SPEC-8(c)) has a writer at all three idle-acquisition sites and the webhook backstops the stamp; the pinned-idle bound (SPEC-8(c)) matches plan.Compute's `warmTarget` clamp; and every pod exit reaches the SPEC-3(a) terminate frame — ALTERNATIVES: filing the §5.2 "When `residualStateWarning: true`, the session runs on a pod that serves more than one session" sentence (rejected: already false before this proposal on `maxSessionsPerPod: 1` and sequential `vm-restart` pools).
- FACT: cancelling the pod MCP surface at occupancy zero closes only the listener. `mcp.Server.Serve` closes `lis` on ctx cancel, and `ServeConn` goroutines keep serving accepted connections, so SPEC-1's "uses the connections the runtime opened on the pod's first session" matches the code. The later session's claim re-arms a fresh server with the new nonce beside them. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/mcp/server.go:137-153, pkg/adapter/slotsession.go:142-165, :397-406
- FACT: agent pods render `RestartPolicy: Never`, so the kubelet does not re-create a stopped runtime container. SPEC-2's "is not re-created inside the pod" holds. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go:980
- FACT: `/tmp` and `/dev/shm` are emptyDir volumes mounted into both the adapter and the runtime container, so SPEC-6(b)'s "steps 0, 2, 4, and 6 operate on volumes mounted into both containers" is true. Step 4 therefore clears the kept runtime's `/tmp` under it, and D4's post-scrub sample is what catches a runtime the scrub breaks. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go:537-557, :646-651
- FACT: Sandboxes carry a controller owner reference to their SandboxWarmPool, so the §10.7 `status.readyCount == 0` deletion (reachable at once when every idle pod is pinned) cascades to pinned idle pods rather than orphaning them. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/controller.go:539; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:1121
- WATCHOUT: `verifyIntegrationLevel` caches the verdict per gateway replica (`integrationVerified`) and reads `mcpHandshakeSeen`, which `takePodMCPCancelsLocked` clears at occupancy zero. A replica whose first assignment for a runtime lands on a kept process's later session may observe a Standard runtime as Basic. This is code-only, and §5.1 places the check at the adapter's first handshake. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/integrationlevel.go:87-114, pkg/adapter/slotsession.go:163
- UNVERIFIED: CODE-8's IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE allows skipping the pin read on a non-recycling pool, and SPEC-8(c) says "every idle-pod acquisition". If a pool is edited from `recycle.enabled: true` to false while pinned idle pods remain, a skipped read hands another tenant's claim to the webhook, and the claim then errors rather than passing over the pod. The non-spec loop should decide whether the choice's constraint is enough. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-8 last bullet; spec-changes.md SPEC-8(c)
- USEFUL [spec.4.review-reliability.1]: the per-Attach heartbeat FACT settled SPEC-14(a) against §15.4.3 Basic without re-reading attach.go.


### [spec.4.review-performance.1]

FACT: `lenny_warmpool_idle_pods` is published from the warm-pool planner's `ReadyCount` (`pkg/controller/warmpool/controller.go:313`, `setIdlePods(pool.Name, decision.ReadyCount)`), and the idle-cost counter observes the same value (`controller.go:305`). CODE-10 removes pinned idle pods from `ReadyCount`, so it silently changes both series. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-10 ("is not counted in `WarmCount` or `ReadyCount`").
FACT: the gateway's claim path uses an uncached client (`cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go:169`, `client.New`), so `Claimer.Claim` already issues a direct Sandbox LIST per claim, and `AdmitTenantPin` adds one direct Pod GET per scanned candidate. No new informer or watch is created on the gateway. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go:104-108.
FACT: the recycle counters (`sessions_served`, `scrub_failure_count`) live in Postgres `agent_pod_state`. A Postgres error during `RecordPodScrub` propagates, so the pod retires through the report timeout (fail closed). The new `Decide` inputs add no Redis dependency. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/leasecontrol/scrubreport_server.go:504-512, :558-564.
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: CODE-10 drops pinned idle pods from `ReadyCount`, and that value also feeds `r.idle.observe` (`lenny_warmpool_idle_pod_minutes`, the idle-cost counter behind `WarmPoolIdleCostHigh`). As staged, pinned idle pods, up to maxWarm minus minWarm per pool, stop counting toward idle cost. Keep `ReadyCount + PinnedIdleCount` in the idle-cost observation. What the gauge should report depends on how the spec finding on the §16.1 `lenny_warmpool_idle_pods` row is resolved.
OPEN: on an `allowCrossTenantReuse` pool, SPEC-8(c) drains every pinned pod that a claim's scan refuses, not only the first. When no admissible pod exists, one claim from another tenant drains the pool's entire pinned idle inventory. It is not reported, because the replacements add unpinned capacity during exhaustion and the result is consistent with D6's "would reach" wording. A human may want "at most one drain per acquisition".

### [spec.4.review-performance.2]

DECISION: no spec-scoped findings under the performance and failure-mode lens in the re-run of round 4. BECAUSE the new write paths add no per-session etcd class beyond what spec/04 §4.6.1 **etcd write pressure at scale** already budgets: an unacknowledged recycling pool, or a bound-zero pinned drain, pays the non-recycling pod replacement plus at most the recycle PATCHes (+~90 writes/s at 30 claims/s) that the paragraph already adds. Pinned plus unpinned idle stays at or below maxWarm, which `pods_floor` sums (spec/17_deployment-topology.md:1105). Every value the design relies on is in etcd, in Postgres, or in adapter memory, none of it in Redis. ALTERNATIVES: filing the tenant-scan order, in which a tenant's claim binds the first admissible pod rather than its own pinned pod. I rejected it because it is a reuse-efficiency loss, and neither the shipped §5.2 text nor the staged text requires a preference.
FACT: the pool reconciler `Owns(&Sandbox{})` (pkg/controller/warmpool/controller.go:998-1004), so every Sandbox event reconciles the pool. spec/04_system-components.md:459 routes every non-create, non-status request ("reads, deletions, finalizer updates") through one 10 QPS / burst 100 limiter, and :461 sets `--max-concurrent-reconciles` to 1 by default. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:454-461
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md CODE-10 / §4.5]: as staged, CODE-10 issues an uncached `APIReader` SandboxClaim Get for every pinned idle pod on every pool reconcile, before `plan.Compute`. Those Gets share the 10 QPS bucket with drain deletions and finalizer updates, and one default worker serializes every pool. With P pinned idle pods, which reach maxWarm − minWarm under D14, each reconcile waits about P/10 s once the burst of 100 is spent. That delays the Create of every pool, so replenishment stalls at Tier 3, where 5–10 hot pools take several Sandbox events per claim at 30 claims/s. A cached NotFound is enough for the counts. Only the pods selected for `DrainPinned` need the uncached confirmation, which the WATCHOUT on the predicate's claim condition says is load-bearing for the drain only. Restrict the uncached Get to the drain candidates, which number len(pinned) − bound and are near zero in steady state.
FACT: when a pool has only pinned idle pods left, other-tenant claims do not run a scan that GETs every pinned pod. PoolWarmingUp (spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:638-646) is True whenever idlePodCount (unpinned only, after SPEC-8(c)) is 0 and replacements are warming, so the gateway returns 503 before the scan. In steady state a claim costs about 1 + P/(U+1) Pod GETs, where U is the unpinned idle count. That is small next to the per-claim Sandbox LIST (claimer.go:104-108).
FACT: `slothealth.Tracker` is in-memory, per gateway replica (pkg/gateway/runtime/slothealth/slothealth.go:55-75). This answers the UNVERIFIED in spec.4.review-reliability.1: the whole-pod trigger for a concurrent pod with a hung or ended runtime counts per replica. The pod is still bounded by the per-release maxSessionsPerPod drain and by the occupancy-zero retire on a non-recycling pool. This predates the proposal.
USEFUL [spec.1.review-performance.1]: its FACTs that the gateway uses an uncached client and that sessions_served lives in Postgres saved re-deriving the claim-path read cost and the Redis-reset survival of the maxSessionsPerPod bound.


### [spec.4.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no reliability finding against the staged spec edits in round 4 — BECAUSE every recovery path the staging relies on already exists and closes within a bound: a sequential failed session retires its pod (§5.2 Recycle lifecycle, "A session that ends in failure or a crash always retires its pod"), a concurrent failed slot start counts toward the whole-pod trigger (sessionserver accountSlotFailure), and a runtime that dies in reserved or idle is found by the next claim's fail-fast Start — ALTERNATIVES: a boundary heartbeat probe, or proactive adapter reporting of `ended`. Both would only recover faster, which is below the bar.
FACT: the reserved-hold dispatch goes through Claimer.rebindReserved inside Claim, so a failed start there reaches Binder.failPhase and drains, the same as the idle path — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go:118,189; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/reclaim.go (failPhase)
FACT: agent pods render no readiness or liveness probe, and the WarmPoolController reads no container status, so an idle pod whose runtime container exited is found only at the next claim — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go (no Probe fields); pkg/controller/warmpool
FACT: the heartbeat monitor runs per Attach session rather than per connection, so SPEC-14(a)'s "ends that session's stream" matches the code — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/attach.go:75,146-151
FACT: the plan Drain goes through patchSandboxDraining with requireIdle=false. If the pinned-idle drain (CODE-10) races a claim, it can drain a pod that a session has just claimed. This is code-level and out of scope for the spec loop — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/controller.go:280-283,641
UNVERIFIED: whether the slothealth.Tracker for the whole-pod replacement trigger is per gateway replica (in-memory). If it is, SPEC-7(a)'s concurrent-pool "pod drains when that trigger fires" is slower across replicas, but it stays bounded. The non-spec loop or the performance lens should check this.

### [spec.4.review-reliability.2]
DECISION: filed one finding, the unstaged §10.1.4 **Hold state timeout** sentence ("it sends `terminate` on the CH-RUNTIMEOPS"), which after SPEC-3(a), SPEC-11(b), and SPEC-12(f) contradicts "a session's teardown sends no graceful-shutdown signal ... writes it once ... when the pod terminates" — BECAUSE the blast-radius rule (b) covers "when `terminate` is sent", the site is absent from the table, and the code path already sends nothing (pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:228) — ALTERNATIVES: reading the hold timeout as pod termination ("Before exiting, the adapter SHOULD ..."). I rejected that reading because D8 puts the send on the SIGTERM handler with reason `eviction`, and onHoldTimeout neither exits nor receives SIGTERM.
FACT: §10.1.4 is the only spec site that puts a `terminate` send on the coordinator-loss path. §28.5.1 `CH-FENCE` (spec/28:340-343) and §29.8 (spec/29:1257-1260) say only "graceful session termination with reason coordinator_lost" — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:58
FACT: the gateway has no non-generated consumer of the CH-ADAPTEREVENTS stream, so D9's empty control-event stamp (RATE_LIMITED, AUTH_EXPIRED, and the rest) loses nothing in the code today. Open decision 1 owns the spec side — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/adapterevents.go:152-155; grep of pkg/gateway for AdapterEvents consumers
WATCHOUT: the only production caller of `slothealth` RecordFailure is the bind-failure path, so a slot that fails after `running`, such as a heartbeat-hung runtime, never counts toward the whole-pod replacement trigger in code. The spec-changes §5 heartbeat row relies on that count. Code gap against §5.2 **Slot retry policy**, not a spec finding — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/slot_bind.go:183
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: the caller directive asks for a test in which a last-slot Interrupt is followed by a sibling Start before the interrupted session's Close, and the Start binds to a live connection. A grep of non-spec-changes.md for such an ordering finds only the inverted halves of the existing Close and Interrupt scoped tests (non-spec-changes.md:131). Those tests do not interleave a sibling Start between the Interrupt and the Close.
USEFUL [spec.1.review-reliability.1]: the FACT that the runtime container has no preStop hook and gets the kubelet SIGTERM at T0 settled that the eviction-path `terminate` adds no new loss of work.


### [spec.4.review-security.1]
DECISION: no security findings on the r3->r4 diff or the rest of the staging — BECAUSE the diff (SPEC-4(g) pointer, SPEC-11(b) diagram, SPEC-12(a)/(b) step tuples, §9.1 wording) touches no control, and every bound the staging introduces has a trusted source: the acknowledgment is read from the Postgres pool record at the boundary (fail-closed on missing pool label, error propagates; pkg/gateway/session/recycle/scrubreporter_seams.go:600-615), sessions_served is a Postgres agent_pod_state column (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:494), the pin is a webhook-guarded Pod label, and runtime_live from the adapter can only retire or allow the reuse the pool already acknowledged — ALTERNATIVES: re-filing the SPEC-13 full-revoke / session-expiry "runtime keeps running" outcome (earlier security rounds rejected it as merely less strict, since concurrent pools already keep the shared runtime); filing the missing ConcurrentWorkspaceCredentialSharing-style pool condition for acknowledged sequential pools (not a removed control; the client disclosure covers it).
FACT: the claim paths fail closed when the tenant stamp fails. Claimer.Claim returns an error after stampPodTenant fails (it tolerates only NotFound), so a TOCTOU between the pin read and the CREATE cannot dispatch a session across tenants: the webhook refuses the B->A relabel and the claim errors. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go:150-157, pkg/admission/label_immutability/label_immutability.go:197-205
USEFUL [spec.3 security DECISION on SPEC-4(d) unassigned reset]: confirmed again that the webhook forbids unassigned->{tenant}, so the ungated WPC reset can make a pod unclaimable but cannot move it across tenants. Do not re-file.
### [spec.4.review-security.1]
- DECISION: no security findings on the round-4 staging — BECAUSE every bound the staged spec edits introduce has a trusted source (the acknowledgment from the Postgres pool record at the boundary, the pin from the gateway-stamped, webhook-guarded Pod label, the session count from Postgres), `runtime_live` from the adapter can only retire a pod or allow reuse the pool already acknowledged (an omitted field reads as false), and the tenant-change drain on `allowCrossTenantReuse` pools tightens the prior cross-tenant reuse posture — ALTERNATIVES: filing SPEC-4(d)'s removal of the per-pool restriction on the WPC `{tenant_id} → unassigned` reset (rejected: the webhook never had it, since it cannot read pool config, §5.2 already states the transition unconditionally, and `unassigned` is refused to every tenant, so the reset exposes nothing); filing SPEC-6(b) "steps 0, 2, 4, and 6 clear the runtime container's view" on cross-UID delete permissions (rejected as speculative and pre-existing; step 6 verification fails closed).
- FACT: a stale-cache pin read in AdmitTenantPin cannot place a different tenant on a pinned pod: `stampPodTenant` then patches A→B, the tenant-label webhook refuses it, and `Claimer.Claim` returns the error — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go:157-159; pkg/admission/label_immutability/label_immutability.go:197-205
- USEFUL [review-log :400 FACT on §11.4 full revoke]: the revoke fan-out sends a non-recycle `Shutdown` (cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go terminateLocal), so the pod takes the default disposition and is replaced; SPEC-13 does not leave a revoked user's kept runtime serving a later session. Re-check only if the revoke path ever sends `ShutdownRecycle`.

### [spec.6.review-performance.1]

- DECISION: no spec-scoped findings under the performance and failure-mode lens in round 6 — BECAUSE the round-5 edits (SPEC-8(e) second replacement, SPEC-10 `podReuse` deletion, SPEC-11(b) `DRAINING` pointer, SPEC-12(a) line-break rule, SPEC-12(f) 4b trim, SPEC-18) add no write path, no read path, and no store dependency. The write-rate math in spec.4.review-performance.2 still holds: an unacknowledged recycling pool or a bound-zero pinned drain costs the non-recycling per-session profile plus about 2 claim PATCHes (+~60 writes/s at the Tier 3 rate of 30 claims/s), within the ~800 writes/s estimate in spec/04 §4.6.1 **etcd write pressure at scale** — ALTERNATIVES: filing the §4.6.1 sentence "On a recycling pool the per-pod claim amortizes CREATE and DELETE traffic" as stale for unacknowledged pools; rejected because the paragraph is an estimate, and an episode of one session keeps it true.
- FACT: SPEC-18 does not strand the runtime at hold timeout within the spec's own model. §10.1.4 has the adapter exit after the hold timeout ("Before exiting, the adapter SHOULD send a final `AdapterTerminating`"), and SPEC-3(a) sends `terminate` "when the pod terminates" — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:58
- UNVERIFIED: the code does not exit at hold timeout (`onHoldTimeout` only terminates each held session; pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:177-211). Its doc comment gives the purpose as "so no agent process is left running with live provider credentials and no coordinator" (:160-162). Today the last member's `Close` ends the sidecar runtime through EOF. After D3 the kept runtime survives the hold timeout until something retires the pod, and `orphansession` acts only on pods already `terminated` (pkg/gateway/session/orphansession/orphansession.go:14-17). The non-spec reliability or security lens should confirm what retires the pod after a hold timeout.
- USEFUL [spec.4.review-performance.2]: its write-rate and claim-path GET math (1 + P/(U+1) Pod GETs per claim; PoolWarmingUp 503 before the scan when only pinned pods are idle) let me skip re-deriving the Tier 3 budget.


### [spec.6.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: returned no findings in round 6 — BECAUSE every recovery path the staged spec relies on was traced and holds: a dead runtime is caught at the boundary sample or by the fail-fast start (StartSession returns codes.Internal, pkg/adapter/session.go:162-167, which is not a slot-bind refusal, so reclaimOnFailure drains, pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/reclaim.go:28-35); the concurrent path counts it through accountSlotFailure (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/slot_bind.go:183-190); the retire inputs are read at report time (InspectForRecycle called from RecordPodScrub, scrubreport_server.go:504); an adapter crash cannot leave a runtime waiting for a redial because agent pods run RestartPolicyNever (pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go:980) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the §29.9 4a/4b order (rejected; see USEFUL below), and filing §10.7 pool deletion racing pinned idle pods (rejected: Sandboxes carry a controller ownerReference to the pool, pkg/controller/warmpool/controller.go:539, so the pool's deletion cascades to them).
USEFUL [spec.5 WATCHOUT on §29.9 4a/4b order]: it saved a false finding. The one real hazard is that CODE-4 sends `terminate` from the SIGTERM goroutine that runPreStop fires (cmd/lenny-adapter/prestop.go:100), which is before the eviction checkpoint's checkpoint_request. The same goroutine already calls lifecycle.Close() at that point in the shipped tree (cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:412-425), so the Full-level eviction checkpoint was broken before this proposal and remains a code-conformance gap.
FACT: scrub step 1 spares the adapter only because the adapter is PID 1 in its container. DefaultOps runs `sh -c "kill -9 -1"` as the adapter's own UID, and kill(-1) exempts init, not the parent — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/scrub/defaultops.go:28-35; cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:85-90. SPEC-6(b)'s "which step 1 spares" depends on this.
FACT: the SDK-warm ConfigureWorkspace path exists only for the in-process runtime (sdkWarmRuntime), so no sidecar pod takes the ConfigureWorkspace start in place of StartSession, and the fail-fast Start covers every sidecar start — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/sdkwarm.go:212-216, :265-268.

### [spec.9.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-8(d) is the one home of what an admitted pool edit that leaves the acknowledgment rule does to pods already kept: it ends no kept runtime process, a `reserved` or pinned-idle pod may serve one more same-tenant session in that process, and the pod retires at its next recycle boundary under the new configuration, or at that session's end when the pool no longer recycles. The §5 edge row "Pool edit that removes the acknowledgment" now keeps only its refuse/admit sentence and cites SPEC-8(d); DOC-4's troubleshooting remedy lost its "so no runtime process is kept across sessions" clause — BECAUSE the earlier SPEC-8(d) categorical ("outside the rule keep no runtime process") was false during the transition — ALTERNATIVES: rescoping the lead in place (it omits the extra same-tenant session in the kept process and the recycle.enabled:false case); restoring a SPEC-7(a) acknowledgment read at the scrub report (re-opens human decision 2, Alternative B); a DOC-4 caveat (adds docs text the finding does not need).
FACT: nothing ends a kept process when a pool edit lands. The boundary re-reads the pool record (`poolRecyclePolicy`, pkg/gateway/session/recycle/scrubreporter_seams.go:681-686), a nil policy (recycle.enabled:false) returns a no-op disposition and the pod retires via the session path (:617-623), and the only session-count check is podscrub `Decide` (pkg/sandbox/podscrub/podscrub.go:399). The SandboxTemplate CRD carries no recycle fields, so no rollout fires. EVIDENCE: files above.
WATCHOUT: do not add a boundary acknowledgment check, a drain on pool edit, or a rollout trigger to end kept processes at the edit. Each is a new mechanism, and the boundary gate lost to CODE-11 admission under human decision 2. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-8(d).
OPEN: DOC-4 states no reader-facing transition caveat for withdrawing the acknowledgment. Whether security-principles.md or troubleshooting.md should say that pods already kept serve one more same-tenant session belongs to the non-spec docs-alignment lens.


### [spec.9.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the transition outcome of an admitted acknowledgment-removing edit has one home, SPEC-8(d); the sentence lands before the "outside the rule" sentence, which gains "Otherwise," — BECAUSE the categorical "keep no runtime process across sessions" is false for a pod already `reserved` or pinned idle when the edit lands, and a deployer withdrawing the acknowledgment acts on it — ALTERNATIVES: rescoping the lead to "retires at its next recycle boundary" (rung 3) leaves the isolation fact (one more session in the kept process) implicit and omits the recycle.enabled:false case; a docs mirror of the transition in DOC-4 adds text the finding does not need.
FACT: nothing ends a kept runtime when a pool edit lands: the claim path never compares sessionsServed to maxSessionsPerPod (the only check is podscrub.Decide at the boundary), and the boundary reads the pool record afresh — EVIDENCE: pkg/sandbox/podscrub/podscrub.go:399; pkg/gateway/session/recycle/scrubreporter_seams.go:617-623 (nil policy on recycle.enabled:false, pod retires through the session path)
DECISION: the §5 edge row "Pool edit that removes the acknowledgment" loses its second sentence and cites SPEC-8(d); the DOC-4 troubleshooting remedy loses "so no runtime process is kept across sessions" — BECAUSE both restate the outcome the fix makes SPEC-8(d) the home of, and the DOC clause becomes false for an update.
OPEN: no reader-facing doc states that pods reused before an acknowledgment-removing update serve one more session in the kept process (DOC-4 security-principles and troubleshooting are silent after the deletion). A later docs-alignment round may judge that a coverage gap.

### [spec.9.review-performance.1]
- DECISION: no spec-scoped findings under the performance and failure-mode lens in round 9 — BECAUSE this is the first performance pass since redesign1.apply.3 (rounds 7 and 8 ran the mechanism lens only), and the apply.3 surfaces add no write path, no read path, and no store dependency: the SPEC-8(d) admission rule runs once per pool write; SPEC-18(b) and the SPEC-2 clause close the connection only at a hold timeout, which §10.1.4 already scopes to the whole pod and which already terminates every session on it, so siblings lose nothing they did not lose before; and the no-terminate rule (SPEC-3(a), SPEC-11(b)) removes a write. The write-rate math in spec.4.review-performance.2 and spec.6.review-performance.1 still holds — ALTERNATIVES: filing the concurrent-pool hung-runtime reuse (per-replica in-memory slothealth, so the whole-pod trigger counts per replica; rejected because the §5 edge-case row accepts it, the shipped baseline is the F-5.2.33 break, and maxSessionsPerPod bounds it); filing the fallback-claim pin read as needing the apiserver (rejected: §4.6.1 Fallback preconditions item 2 already requires apiserver reachability for the fallback, so a Pod GET adds no new failure condition).
- FACT: the coordinator hold arms only when the pod has a started session (`onCoordinatorChannelClosed` returns on `!s.hasStartedSession()`), so a gateway rolling restart never runs the CODE-4 teardown on an idle or `reserved` pod that keeps a runtime — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:90-99
- FACT: `plan.Compute` drains unpinned idle down to the warm target (min(minWarm, maxWarm)), so SPEC-8(c)'s pinned bound of maxWarm minus that target keeps pinned plus unpinned idle at or below maxWarm — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/plan/plan.go:131-145, :151-163
- USEFUL [spec.4.review-performance.2]: the Tier 3 write-rate figures and the PoolWarmingUp-before-scan reasoning let me skip re-deriving the claim-path budget.
- USEFUL [spec.9.review-single-source.1]: its statement that round 6's passes predate apply.3 told me which surfaces had not yet had a performance pass.


### [spec.9.review-reliability.1]
FACT: agent pods run `RestartPolicy: Never` (pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go:980), so a stopped sidecar runtime container is never restarted by the kubelet; D4's sticky `ended` with no redial matches the pod's real lifecycle rather than giving up a recovery path. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go:980
FACT: §5.2 already retires the pod on any session that ends in failure or a crash, regardless of recycle settings (spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:471, :508). That is what bounds the hung-runtime case on `maxConcurrentSessions: 1` pools (spec-changes §5, row "Runtime misses a heartbeat") and the post-hold-timeout pod, because the orphan session reconciler fails the terminated sessions. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:471
FACT: the coordinator hold is pod-scoped (one AdapterEvents stream per pod; `enterHoldState` names no session), so the D10 pod-scope teardown at `onHoldTimeout` does not cut off a sibling session whose coordinator is still alive. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:100-127
FACT: the concurrent failed-start retire in SPEC-7(a) is the existing `accountSlotFailure` threshold (ceil(maxConcurrentSessions/2) in a rolling window, plus leaks). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/slot_bind.go:143-190
DECISION: no reliability findings on the spec staging in round 9 — BECAUSE the round-8 diff only relocates SPEC-4(g), and the recovery paths (runtime_not_live report, failed-start drain, hold-timeout teardown, tenant-change drain, pinned-idle drain) each have a reclaimer that holds up under restart. The fallback claim's pin read requires apiserver reachability, which §4.6.1 fallback precondition 2 already demands (spec/04_system-components.md:444) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the "Before exiting" wording in §10.1.4 against D10's "adapter keeps running"; rejected because standing context records adapter exit at the timeout as a pre-existing gap.


### [spec.9.review-security.1]
FACT: the tenant-label webhook authorizes `{tenant_id} -> unassigned` for the WarmPoolController SA on every pool; it reads no pool config. So SPEC-4(d) dropping "only on a pool whose ... allowCrossTenantReuse" aligns §4.6.3 with §5.2's Kubernetes-layer item and the code, and it relaxes nothing. — EVIDENCE: pkg/admission/label_immutability/label_immutability.go:185-195; spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:445
FACT: the recycle disposition's sessions-served input is advanced by the adapter's ReportSessionScrub, which is an in-pod report and is not measured independently by the gateway. This predates 0079. The runtime cannot forge it, and the remedy would be code, so it was not filed. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/leasecontrol/scrubreport_server.go:534-540, :461
FACT: `runtime_live` cannot extend a security bound. A false or omitted value retires the pod. The limit, scrub-failure, and uptime retires are evaluated before it (SPEC-7(a), "after every retirement above"), so a self-reported `true` only preserves today's disposition.
OPEN: on an acknowledged recycling pool, a full revoke (SPEC-13) and a budget or expiry end leave the kept runtime running the ended session's in-flight work with no signal. In direct mode the provider key stays in its memory. Edge case "Runtime at a session end" and decisions 3 and 5 settle this, and the runtime-SDK follow-up's end-of-session signal is the remedy. Do not file it in this loop, because it re-opens D3 and D8.
USEFUL [step-1b DECISION at review-log :597-599]: the best-effort parenthetical "(shmget ... addressed by step 1b above.)" is kept on purpose as a pointer. That entry saved re-filing it as a missed edit site.

### [spec.11.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: closed the SPEC-8(d) transition under-warn by disclosure: deleted D12's clause "which covers every pool on which a runtime process can serve more than one session", added one sentence to SPEC-8(d) after "or at that session's end on a pool that no longer recycles" stating that the session's `sessionIsolationLevel` follows the updated pool configuration (§7.1) so `residualStateWarning` can be `false`, and cut the §5 edge-case `sessionIsolationLevel` row to a pointer at D12 and SPEC-8(d) — BECAUSE §7.1 computes the fields from the pool at create (isolationlevel.go `scrub != ""` gate; create.go resolveIsolationLevel) and rebindReserved/reservedClaimForTenant read no recycle setting, so the kept-process transition session on a pool with recycle disabled and maxConcurrentSessions 1 reports false — ALTERNATIVES: fail-closed acquisition refusal or drain on pool edit (new mechanism; spec.9.fix-G1.1 rejected pool-edit drains; config race at create still under-warns); refusing an update that disables recycling (validateRecyclePolicy sees only the new pool); deleting the transition sentence (reopens the round-9 finding).
WATCHOUT: do not write that the transition session "reports false". With maxConcurrentSessions > 1 and recycling disabled it still reports true. SPEC-8(d) says "can be false" and points to §7.1 for the values. SPEC-8(d) is the one home of this under-warn; do not restate it in D12, SPEC-8(e), or SPEC-10. — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:73, :75; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/isolationlevel.go:208-212
OPEN: a pod that served an earlier session is admitted to a session whose persisted `scrubPolicy` is empty (pre-existing: rebindReserved reads no recycle setting, and before CODE-8 a recycled idle pod went to any tenant with the warning false). The race-free fail-closed fix (admit such a pod only to a session with non-empty scrubPolicy) is broader than 0079 and is for a human to file separately. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go rebindReserved, reservedClaimForTenant


### [spec.11.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: close the SPEC-8(d) transition under-warn by disclosure: delete D12's clause ", which covers every pool on which a runtime process can serve more than one session", add one sentence to the SPEC-8(d) staged block saying the transition session's `sessionIsolationLevel` follows the updated pool configuration (§7.1 link), so on a pool that no longer recycles its `residualStateWarning` can be `false`, and cut the §5 edge row `sessionIsolationLevel` to a pointer at D12 and SPEC-8(d) — BECAUSE the under-warn at a recycle-disabling update predates this proposal for the pod-level vectors (rebindReserved reads no recycle setting, and before CODE-8 a recycled idle pod went to any tenant), the session is same-tenant only (pin plus reservedClaimForTenant), and spec.9.fix-G1.1 already ruled out drains or gates at pool edit — ALTERNATIVES: (1) refuse and drain a pinned or reserved pod at acquisition when the pool no longer recycles (new ClaimRequest field, AdmitTenantPin clause, rebindReserved guard, TEST-12 cases); rejected as new mechanism for a pre-existing gap, and it still races because the level is persisted at create (create.go resolveIsolationLevel) while the bind reads the pool later; (2) refuse an update that disables recycle (validateRecyclePolicy sees only the new pool, needs an old/new comparison and restricts operators); (3) report reuse at bind (the level is persisted on the row at create, before any claim).
FACT: §7.1 `sessionIsolationLevel` is computed once at session creation from the pool record and persisted on the session row; the claim happens later and does not revisit it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/create.go:225-250 (resolveIsolationLevel, persisted ScrubPolicy); pkg/gateway/sessionserver/isolationlevel.go:208-212
FACT: the reserved rebind checks only phase, claim binding state, tenant, and holdExpiresAt; no recycle setting. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go rebindReserved, reservedClaimForTenant
OPEN: pre-existing and outside this proposal: a session created after a pool stops recycling can bind a `reserved` or pinned idle pod that served an earlier session while its persisted level reports `residualStateWarning: false`. A fail-closed fix would admit a pod that served an earlier session only to a session whose persisted `scrubPolicy` is non-empty, in rebindReserved and AdmitTenantPin. A human decides whether to file it as a separate BUILD-GAPS finding.
OPEN: no docs/ page mirrors the SPEC-8(d) transition, including the new under-warn sentence; docs/client-guide/session-lifecycle.md tells strict-isolation clients to check `residualStateWarning`. The non-spec docs-alignment lens weighs whether DOC-5 owes a mirror (spec.10.review-docs-alignment.1 found no docs page on the pool-update path).

### [spec.11.review-performance.1]
- DECISION: no spec-scoped findings under the performance and failure-mode lens in round 11 — BECAUSE the only change since r9 is the SPEC-8(d) clause on an update that takes a pool outside the rule (and the matching edge-table reduction), which adds no write, read, or store dependency; the clause is consistent with the gateway reading the current pool record at disposition time (pkg/gateway/session/recycle/scrubreporter_seams.go:681-686, `poolRecyclePolicy`), so a `reserved` or pinned idle pod retires at its next boundary or, on a pool that no longer recycles, at session end — ALTERNATIVES: filing SPEC-8(c) bound shifts on each PoolScalingController `minWarm` write as drain thrash (rejected: drains only pinned pods above the bound, and total pods stay at or below `maxWarm`); refiling the pinned-tenant PoolWarmingUp 503 (accepted in the edge table).
- FACT: the SPEC-8(d) "outside the rule" clause has one statement; the edge-case table row now cites SPEC-8(d) rather than restating it, and neither non-spec staging nor the summary repeats it — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:97, :346-350
- USEFUL [spec.9.review-performance.1]: its write-rate and hold-timeout reasoning still holds and let me skip re-deriving the Tier 3 budget.


### [spec.11.review-reliability.1]
FACT: the new SPEC-8(d) update sentence holds against the code. `InspectForRecycle` re-reads the pool from the pool store at every boundary (`pkg/gateway/session/recycle/scrubreporter_seams.go:607-623`), so a `maxSessionsPerPod: 1` or `vm-restart` edit applies at the next boundary. `BindResult.Recycle` is resolved from the pool mirror at bind time (`pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resolve.go:376`, `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/pool_policy_mirror.go:85-93`), so a session that binds after a `recycle.enabled: false` edit retires at its end through `release.go:77`. EVIDENCE: those lines.
FACT: a stopped runtime cannot stall a non-recycling concurrent pod. `SlotClaimer.ReleaseSlot` deletes the per-pod claim at occupancy zero when `recycle` is false (`pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go:881-885`), so the pod retires once its failing slots drain. On a recycling concurrent pod the occupancy-zero report carries `runtime_live: false`. EVIDENCE: slotclaimer.go:845-885.
FACT: agent pods use `RestartPolicyNever` (`pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go:980`), so a runtime or adapter container is never restarted inside the pod. The in-memory sticky `ended` is authoritative for the pod's life, and no restarted adapter can see a fresh `connected=false` state.
FACT: the hold arms only when a session has started (`pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:89-95`), and fence and timer are mutually exclusive under `hold.mu` (`:177-187`, `exitHoldState`). The "fence lands first" edge row holds.
WATCHOUT: on a concurrent recycling pool, a hung runtime (connection open, no heartbeat acks) is reported live and reused until the §5.2 whole-pod replacement trigger fires. That trigger needs ceil(N/2) failures in a 5-minute window. Edge row 80 and SPEC-3(c) accept this explicitly, and per-session failures still count toward maxSessionsPerPod, so the case is slower recovery rather than a stall. Do not file it as a reliability finding. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:577 (Whole-pod replacement trigger).
UNVERIFIED: for the security lens. On a recycling pool, §11.4 full-revoke step 3 (SPEC-13) tears down the revoked user's session but leaves the runtime process, with any direct-mode credential copy in its memory, serving the tenant's next session. SPEC-9(i) and SPEC-15 acknowledge credential retention in a kept process. Nobody has checked whether a `USER_REVOKED` Shutdown should take the default (replace) disposition rather than recycle.


### [spec.11.review-security.1]
- DECISION: filed one finding. The round-10 SPEC-8(d) sentence accepts a session in a kept runtime process on a pool an update took out of recycling, and that contradicts D12's claim that the disclosure fields are `true` on every pool where a runtime process can serve more than one session — BECAUSE `sessionIsolationLevel` is computed from the current pool configuration (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/isolationlevel.go:208-212), and `rebindReserved` and the idle loop read no recycle setting (pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go:118, :189-235), so the transition session reports `podReuse: false` and `residualStateWarning: false` — ALTERNATIVES: filing the residual-state under-warning (DNS, page cache) on that same transition. Rejected, because it predates 0079. Only the kept-process vector is new.
- FACT: the tenant-pin backstop holds even where the gateway skips the pin read. `stampPodTenant` runs on every claim and treats only NotFound and Conflict as benign, so the webhook's 403 on `{A} -> {B}` fails the claim before dispatch. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go:64-76; pkg/admission/label_immutability/label_immutability.go:197-205
- FACT: `VMRestart` is derived only from `scrubProfile` (pkg/gateway/session/recycle/scrubreporter_seams.go:439, :639). A runc pool that sets `scrubProfile: vm-restart` to escape the SPEC-8(d) rule therefore retires at every boundary, and it is not a bypass.
- DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: CODE-8's IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE ("whether to skip the Pod read on a non-recycling pool. Any answer must read the pin on every recycling pool's candidate") is too weak after SPEC-8(d)'s transition sentence. A pool that an update took out of recycling can still hold pinned idle pods with kept runtimes. Skipping the read there sends a different tenant's claim to such a pod, where the webhook refuses the stamp. On the primary path that fails the create, and leaks the claim per §9.1, instead of passing over to the next candidate as SPEC-8(c) states ("passes over a pod the pin refuses to the next idle candidate"). The constraint should cover every pool, or the choice should be dropped.
- USEFUL [spec.9.review-security.1 FACT on the tenant-label webhook and on the ReportSessionScrub count]: both saved re-deriving and re-filing.

- USEFUL [spec.9.review-single-source.1 DECISION]: its per-rule home inventory held unchanged this round, so only the one changed hunk needed a fresh sweep.
